# Sections

Flat index of every section file in the library. The primary entry point is the [topics index](../topics/README.md) for concept-driven queries or the [sources index](../sources/README.md) for source-driven queries; this page exists as a backstop for "did the library ingest section X" lookups.

Sections are named `<source-slug>--<section-slug>.md`. Sorting by filename groups all sections derived from one source.

§completes-the-§xs-worker-capability-trio with cycle 178 snapshot + cycle 182 debugger sharing cycle 176 endor substrate; §the eighteenth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-184; **single most structurally interesting move**: §admission-control-eliminates-embargo combined with §budget-as-pre-payment-not-post-payment — ensure budget ≥ worst-case-cost (hard_limit) before delivery so any normally-completing crank is fully paid for, with no need for outbound message buffering / rollback / embargo; §the-§Key-Insight section names the realization explicitly: "earlier revision proposed embargoing... a simpler model avoids embargo entirely"; §the-§Prompt section preserves BOTH the rejected embargo formulation AND the §design-evolution-realization ("It just occurred to me there is a simpler way"); §design-evolution-record-in-prompt-section (sibling to cycle 178 §revised-scope-discussion-2026-04-15 + cycle 180 §design-after-implementation-as-ratification + cycle 183 "Initialization is often awkward"); §three-modes-as-discriminated-union (Measurement default / Quota / Rate-limited); §two-thread-architecture (supervisor tokio owns MeterState + admission gate; XS thread owns meterIndex + CRANK_LIMIT thread-local + safety-net callback); §hard-limit-as-termination-not-pause (Decision 2) — XS state after metering abort unreliable; supervisor re-creates worker from cycle 178 snapshot infrastructure; §xs-worker-trio-cohesion (metering depends on snapshot's re-creation path); §budget-as-pre-payment-not-post-payment (Decision 5) — budget represents *available* steps subtracted after each crank; actual cost often less than worst case; §lazy-rate-limit-refill (Decision 3) — compute on demand when message arrives or query made; no background timer; §ready_time-as-tokio-scheduling-hint enables `sleep_until` single wake-up rather than polling; §the-§third-flavor between cycle 156's GC-driven and cycle 173's known-event-immediate (compute-when-asked); §burst-ceiling-prevents-budget-hoarding (Decision 4) — `.min(burst)` clamps long-idle accumulation as §structural-bound-not-runtime-decision; §measurement-only-as-default (Decision 6) — always-on with limit=0 = zero overhead except callback at interval=10000 ≈ one function call per ~10000 bytecode dispatches; §meter-config-once-not-per-crank (Decision 7) — admission gate lives entirely in supervisor; worker only needs hard limit for safety-net callback; §seven-Design-Decisions in §canonical-Design-Decisions-format matching cycle 182's seven + cycle 180's eight + cycle 178's six; §five-control-verbs (meter-query / meter-reset / meter-set-quota / meter-set-rate / meter-refill daemon→supervisor) + §meter-config (supervisor→worker once) + §meter-report (worker→supervisor per-crank with steps+outcome ok/terminated) = §seven-envelope-verbs total; §all-payloads-CBOR-maps targeting handle 0; §matches-cycle-176-byte-identical-CBOR-envelopes; §seven-phases-all-Complete (9 metering unit tests + 11 codec round-trip + integration tests); §custom-fxAbort C helper (longjmp for recoverable aborts instead of `exit()`) is the §non-obvious-C-contribution making §hard-limit-termination recoverable at supervisor level; §fxRunPromiseJobsMetered raw setjmp guard; §thread-local CRANK_LIMIT no mutex needed (only worker thread reads/writes); §snapshot-integration Phase 6: SuspendedWorker.meter field preserves MeterState across suspend/resume; §restore_meter on resume recomputes last_refill to "now" to §avoid-crediting-idle-time-during-suspension; §JS-manager-integration Phase 7: controlPowers extended with meterQuery / meterReset / meterSetQuota / meterSetRate / meterRefill methods; §six-known-gaps-honestly-disclosed (default metering interval / memory metering / nested calls / hard_limit value choice / snapshot-before-termination forensics / Instant::now() time source across daemon restarts); §three-Dependencies in table (daemon-endor-architecture parent + daemon-xs-worker-snapshot sibling + daemon-rust-xs-performance sibling); §exploit-a-pre-condition-to-eliminate-a-mechanism pattern (sibling to cycle 182's §exploit-the-pre-jump-window — debugger exploits pre-jump-window to avoid backtracking; metering exploits pre-delivery budget-sufficient to avoid embargo); §three-mechanisms-eliminated by admission-control invariant (embargo + rollback + outbound-buffering); §the-§key-simplification declared-as-key in design body; §tier-1 borrowing: §admission-control-eliminates-embargo + §exploit-a-pre-condition-to-eliminate-a-mechanism + §budget-as-pre-payment-not-post-payment + §hard-limit-as-termination-not-pause + §three-mode-meter + §named-modes-as-discriminated-union + §lazy-rate-limit-refill + §ready_time-as-tokio-scheduling-hint + §burst-ceiling-prevents-budget-hoarding + §meter-config-once-not-per-crank + §custom-fxAbort-via-longjmp + §design-evolution-record-in-prompt-section; §synthesis-target: slot machine library worker-layer quota/rate-limiting can §borrow-the-three-mode-meter + §admission-control-pre-payment + §lazy-rate-limit-refill + §burst-ceiling-prevents-hoarding directly; §xs-worker-capability-trio-architecture (snapshot + debugger + metering as sibling capabilities sharing supervisor bus) is §borrowable-architectural-pattern wherever multiple worker-level capabilities share a bus)

### From endo packages/panic/{index.js,README.md} (Cycle 197 chat-lane; 75 + 58 = 133 lines; §thirty-first consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-197; §seventeenth-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family; **single most structurally interesting move**: §three-layer-dispatch-chain-as-imperfect-ponyfill + §Eval-Twin-defense-via-registered-symbol (modeled on PassStyleOfEndowmentSymbol of @endo/pass-style) + §infinite-regress-defense (`panic !== globalThis.panic` identity check in Moddable-XS branch) + §throw-rather-than-infinite-loop with §two-reasons-stacked rationale (CI-pain + browser-spec-violation-makes-infinite-loop-unsafe-too); §three-layer dispatch ordering: (0) `globalThis.console.error('Panic', err)` always if available; (1) `globalThis[PanicEndowmentSymbol]` (registered symbol `Symbol.for('@endo panic')` — the Eval-Twin-safe-delegation-path; expected to be provided by `@agoric/swingset-liveslots`; see [Agoric/agoric-sdk PR #11173](https://github.com/Agoric/agoric-sdk/pull/11173)); (2) `globalThis.process.abort()` (Node); (3) `globalThis.panic(err)` if `typeof === 'function' && !== panic` (Moddable XS with infinite-regress defense); (4) `throw lastResortError`; §the-`@endo-panic`-package-as-imperfect-ponyfill — the README's very first sentence calls it "imperfect"; §ponyfill-vs-shim distinction explicitly named (ponyfill does not modify primordial intrinsics; shim does); §two-stage-rollout-discipline: ponyfill-first-then-shim, with `panic-shim` export forecast for when TC39 proposal advances; §Eval-Twin-Problem ([endojs/endo#1583](https://github.com/endojs/endo/issues/1583)) explicitly cited; §registered-symbol-via-`Symbol.for` chosen over §novel-subclass-extending-Error because subclass identity differs across twin copies; §two-identity-checks-with-explicitly-named-trade-offs: §forgeable-but-twin-safe (the `[PanicEndowmentSymbol]` property on `lastResortError`; anyone can spoof but works across twin copies) vs §non-forgeable-but-twin-vulnerable (`===` to imported `lastResortError`; not spoofable but may produce false-negatives across twins); §infinite-regress-defense via `panic !== globalThis.panic` identity check anticipates a future shim that installs this ponyfill at `globalThis.panic`; §Eval-Twins-as-the-shim-coordination-mechanism — first-to-load-wins-and-installs, all other twins defer (`"defer to the whose shim ran first"` — typo in source); §throw-rather-than-infinite-loop with §two-reasons-stacked: (1) §CI-and-manual-testing-pain — infinite loop is *worse* than thrown error for developers (developer-experience argument given equal weight to security argument); (2) §browser-spec-violation-makes-infinite-loop-unsafe-too — some browsers cap infinite loop at timeout and resume user-code, so even higher-fidelity alternative isn't actually higher fidelity on those engines; §caveat-emptor-at-the-end of README — "should cope with this possibility of the resumption of user-mode execution as best they can"; §prepare-commit-transactional-pattern as README's canonical worked example: §prepare-phase (no side effects, may early-exit by return/throw — the "none" of all-or-none); §try-commit-phase (straight-line no-control-flow so set-of-side-effects-is-clear — the "all" of all-or-none); §catch-panic-phase (`panic(Error(\`unrecoverable transaction fail due to ${err}\`))` on unrecoverable corrupt state must-not-be-observable); §panic-as-the-escape-hatch-for-when-commit-detects-impossible-state pattern matches cycle 162's Ken-properties §atomic-checkpoint and cycle 194's daemon-endo-rust-sqlite §re-prepare-instead-of-caching-Statement — three different libraries / three different languages / three implementations of the same §all-or-none-transactional-discipline; §default-erroneous-exit + no-ambient-normal-exit asymmetry: §erroneous-exit-is-ambient (after team's stance changed); §normal-exit-must-be-capability-granted (because process-spawning-and-graceful-shutdown-are-rights-not-defaults); §the-§"no-further-loss-in-security"-argument: §if-the-untrusted-party-can-already-DoS-via-infinite-loop, §denying-them-erroneous-exit-as-well-buys-nothing; §honest-design-evolution recorded explicitly: "Before this proposal, we had been treating the ability to erroneously exit as an explicit privilege as well. But we are not in a position to prevent user code from going into an infinite loop, which is at least as bad as an erroneous exit. Thus, there is no further loss in security by providing an ambient `panic` operation." — §retroactive-justification-paragraph that names the prior position before naming the new one; sibling to cycles 178/180/183/184/188/192/196 §honest-design-evolution-record family; §Object.freeze discipline on both `panic` and `lastResortError` (§freeze-but-not-harden — preparing-for-stabilize-doc rationale; sibling to cycle 146-E.js §freeze-but-not-harden-the-proxy-target); §two-thirds-prose-one-third-code comment density — of 75 lines of index.js, ~25 are JSDoc/comment, ~17 blank, ~33 executable; plus 58 README lines for total prose-to-code roughly 5:2; §the-knowledge-density-is-deliberately-in-the-text-not-the-tokens; §three-named-future-extensions: (1) shim-export deferred until TC39 advances; (2) platform-specific-immediate-exit-on-other-platforms; (3) Moddable-XS-print-function-detection (TODO comment names §can't-reliably-distinguish-XS-print-from-browser-print); §cross-package-composition named in README: optional upgrade path that uses `@endo/errors` `makeError(X\`...\`${q(err)})` for §better-diagnostic-on-the-ses-console; §two-exports-besides-panic: `PanicEndowmentSymbol` for embedder-installs-handler; `lastResortError` for identity-check-with-named-tradeoffs; §`Object.freeze(panic)` + `Object.freeze(lastResortError)` for §non-trapdoor-immutability without depending on SES whitelist; §the-comment-named-honest-uncertainty in TODO: "use Moddable XS print function if we can reliably distinguish it from `print` in browsers"; §five-line-control-flow-table: layer 0 logging is orthogonal to termination (always runs first); layers 1-4 are mutually-exclusive else-if branches; §canonical-use-case in README: §prepare-commit-with-panic-on-mid-commit-throw; §security-rationale-paragraph distinguishes §ambient-erroneous-exit (allowed because infinite-loop-already-as-bad) from §ambient-normal-exit (denied because §process-spawning-and-graceful-shutdown-are-rights); §SECURITY.md present (Agoric HackerOne + warner-gpg key); §package-version-history: v0.2.0 introduced 2025-06-02 via PR #2815; v1.0.1 released 2025-06-17 as version-only-bump; §tier-1 borrowing: §three-layer-dispatch-chain-as-imperfect-ponyfill + §Eval-Twin-defense-via-registered-symbol following PassStyleOfEndowmentSymbol precedent + §two-identity-checks-with-named-trade-offs (forgeable+twin-safe vs non-forgeable+twin-vulnerable) + §infinite-regress-defense via thisFn !== globalThis.fn + §throw-rather-than-infinite-loop-with-reasoned-justification + §ponyfill-vs-shim-distinction with §two-stage-rollout-discipline + §prepare-commit-transactional-pattern with §panic-as-mid-commit-escape + §default-erroneous-exit + no-ambient-normal-exit asymmetry with §"no-further-loss-in-security" argument + §honest-design-evolution-in-the-README + §two-thirds-prose-one-third-code + §caveat-emptor-at-the-end + §freeze-but-not-harden + §cross-package-composition optional upgrade + §roadmap-in-the-README with §what-blocks-them; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §panic-equivalent for §impossible-state (integrity hash mismatch / commit pre-condition failure / replica divergence) §do-not-resume-as-if-nothing-is-wrong; §three-layer-dispatch-chain pattern adapts directly: registered-symbol-delegation-first (let embedder install handler), platform-immediate-exit-second, throw-with-identity-check as documented imperfect fallback; §Round-2 2026-05-20 reshaped `diagnose(err, options)` (error-post-processor needing try/catch) into `diagnose({ specimen, pattern })` (non-throwing matcher mirroring `matches()`) and split renderer into compact-default + expanded-opt-in; §Round-3 2026-05-20 retired sibling-package framing in favor of `@endo/patterns/explain-mismatch.js` submodule with direct `matchHelpers` registry access dissolving §drift-vs-stable-internal-surface tension, and folded `diagnose`+`render` API into single `explainMismatch({specimen, pattern, context?, format?, width?, color?})` returning rendered string; §three-revision-rounds shrank-the-scope-not-expanded-it; §each-review-round-was-a-simplification not feature-addition; §honest-design-evolution-record family member at higher fidelity than cycle 197 panic README (three pivots visible vs one); §the-data-is-already-there-just-locked is the §central-discovery: `applyLabelingError` in @endo/common already records per-level labels as distinct Error objects forming a cause chain via SES `annotateError` (= `assert.note`); the chain is unreachable to programmatic readers (lives in SES-internal weakmaps, surfaced by console.error but not on err.message or any enumerable property); §the-gap-is-render-not-record is the load-bearing reframing; §discovery-driven-redesign: "build a sibling package that reads what is already there and renders it richly" instead of "thread structured payloads through the matcher"; §what-was-already-true (per-level labels preserved as distinct Errors / SES console renders full causal chain / InterfaceGuard argument labeling composes through the same chain — explain-mismatch gets exo composition for free); §what-was-the-gap (no programmatic walker / no step-kind discrimination — label of `2` could be array index or bag-count index or alternative branch / no combinator awareness — M.or discards branch attempts / no rendering convention beyond flat colon-joined message); §non-throwing-matcher mirroring `matches(specimen, pattern): boolean` — `explainMismatch({...}): string | undefined`; §caller-pattern-collapses-to-single-conditional (`const r = explainMismatch({specimen, pattern}); if (r !== undefined) console.error(r);`); §no-try/catch + §no-error-construction + §no-error-inspection + §no-separate-render-step; §two-Endo-buys-on-the-same-axis: matches() returning boolean is the precedent; explainMismatch returning string|undefined is the extension — both non-throwing; §opt-in-submodule with cost-asymmetry: production matcher path (`mustMatch`, `assertMatches`, `matches`) pays §zero-additional-cost; bundlers and Node ESM loader pull a submodule only when an import names it; §callers-that-never-import-it-never-pay-for-it; §package.json exports simply lists the new entry alongside main; §submodule-direct-access-to-matchHelpers — direct access without re-export hoops; §there-is-exactly-one-source-of-matcher-truth; §why-not-sibling (round-3): sibling would either re-implement matcher (drift) or expose stable internal surface (API contract package would otherwise not need); §why-not-thread-payloads-through-matcher (round-1): production matcher loaded by every application using `mustMatch`/`assertMatches`/`InterfaceGuard`; §a-diagnostic-facility-that-adds-even-a-few-kilobytes-to-the-production-matcher-path-is-a-regression-for-callers-who-never-read-the-resulting-message; §the-three-rejected-framings each had a specific cost that named its rejection; §rich-not-configurable rendering convention with §Rust-compiler-error-analogy as cited prior art: §"Rust's mismatch output does not let the caller pick between 'first candidate' or 'best candidate'; it shows them all, with the most likely intent surfaced as a `help:` suggestion. The explain-mismatch submodule follows that convention."; §all-alternatives-reported-no-heuristic-suppresses-the-others is load-bearing; M.or over three alternatives shows all three branches with attempted-match chain, ranked by depth-of-match (deeper = considered closer), and §the-reader-picks; §rich-rather-than-configurable trades small-verbosity for predictability — §the-same-mismatch-always-renders-the-same-way regardless of how caller configured the package; §a-configurable-picker-would-invite-per-consumer-config-drift; §two-consumer-postures named explicitly: §library-users writing M.splitRecord shapes against incoming CapTP traffic + §AI-agents constructing patterns from natural-language or JSON-Schema inputs; §AI-agents-cannot-walk-the-specimen-interactively is the §unique-cost AI agents pay (humans have REPL escape hatches AI agents don't); §the-design-defends-the-token-budget-by-default: §compact-default-is-AI-tuned (agent has less budget per token than human; one-line-per-mismatch is smaller and easier to line-grep); §expanded-opt-in-is-human-tuned (indented Rust-compiler-style for humans at a REPL); §default-favors-the-tighter-budget-consumer (AI agents) rather than the looser (humans); §pipe-separated-columns `path | found | expected | reason` not JSON-Lines (no key names per line, no quoting overhead; equally machine-parseable: one-line `split(' | ')` recovers columns); §JSON-Lines considered and rejected — §a-consumer-that-genuinely-wants-JSON-gets-a-future-second-export, not §a-configuration-knob-on-the-string-renderer; §ASCII not unicode for terminal/log/CI compatibility; §future-`style: 'unicode'`-option-is-trivial-to-add (line-art characters are module constants) if a caller asks; §seven-trace-step-kinds discriminated-union: property/index/mapKey/setElement/orBranch/arrayOfElement/recordOfEntry for §step-kind-discrimination string/number labels lacked; §tracing-recursion-reuses-helpers-in-place — recursion imports `matchHelpers` and per-combinator helpers directly; §a-change-to-a-helper-updates-both-lanes-simultaneously; §a-shared-test-corpus-exercising-both-`matches`-and-`explainMismatch`-pins-the-verdict-equivalence; §the-earlier-sibling-package-draft-worried-about-drift; §the-submodule-reshape-dissolves-that-concern-entirely; §drift-elimination-by-co-location is round-3's architectural pay-off; §composition with `@endo/exo` argument guards: optional `context` field on `ExplainMismatchInput` carries method-name + argument-index prefix; §no-change-to-@endo/exo-itself; §a-future-helper `explainExoCall(interfaceGuard, methodName, args)` can sugar this when submodule sees real use; §the-primitive-surface-is-`explainMismatch({specimen, pattern, context?})`; §future-helpers-named-not-shipped (sibling to cycle 197 panic §three-named-future-extensions); §nine-Design-Decisions canonical format (sibling to cycles 184/188/192/194/196): 1) submodule-of-@endo/patterns-not-sibling-package, 2) explainMismatch-is-non-throwing-matcher-not-error-post-processor, 3) public-surface-is-single-function-returning-string-not-split-diagnose-plus-render-pair, 4) compact-is-default-format-expanded-is-opt-in, 5) column-separator-is-`|`-not-JSON, 6) all-alternatives-reported-no-closest-alternative-heuristic, 7) tracing-recursion-reuses-matcher's-helpers-in-place, 8) no-text-source-pattern-parser-in-this-design, 9) renderer-uses-ASCII-not-unicode-box-drawing; §each-Design-Decision-names-the-alternative-it-rejected (not "here's what we did" but "here's what we considered and why we chose one"); §single-PR-scope (~600 lines including tests): Phase A = whole-thing; §three-revision-rounds-shrunk-the-scope is unusual and worth naming as §borrowable-pattern; §no-design-predecessors row in Dependencies table — §explicit-no-predecessors as §negative-space-as-record; §sibling-to cycle 192 §implicit-supersedes-lesson-learned but the inverse — when there are no predecessors, §record-that-explicitly so future readers don't search for one; §one-Open-Question (sugar-helper-ships-now-or-waits-for-in-repo-user) — §single-Open-Question discipline is §design-maturity-signal: late in lifecycle; sibling to cycle 196 endoclaw's §seven-Open-Questions for §reference-document vs §198's §one-Open-Question for §nearly-ready-implementation; §tier-1 borrowing: §three-revision-pivots-visible-in-Prompt-section + §the-data-is-already-there-just-locked discovery + §non-throwing-matcher mirroring an existing non-throwing API shape + §opt-in-submodule with cost-asymmetry + §submodule-not-sibling-package + §rich-not-configurable rendering convention + §all-alternatives-reported-no-heuristic + §two-consumer-postures named explicitly + §compact-default + expanded-opt-in with §default-favors-the-tighter-budget-consumer + §pipe-separated-columns + §ASCII-not-unicode + §tracing-recursion-reuses-helpers-in-place + §seven-trace-step-kinds discriminated-union + §each-Design-Decision-names-the-alternative-it-rejected + §nine-Design-Decisions canonical format + §single-PR-scope-despite-three-revision-rounds + §explicit-no-predecessors-row + §future-helpers-named-not-shipped + §single-Open-Question-discipline + §composition-without-modification; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §pattern-matcher-diagnostic-surface can §borrow-the-non-throwing-matcher-shape directly (a `explainOutcome({outcome, predicate}): string | undefined` mirror of a boolean `matches()` API); §opt-in-submodule discipline gates the diagnostic feature behind explicit import so production payout-path pays zero; §the-data-is-already-there-just-locked discovery pattern borrowable in any §library-with-a-throwing-API that consumers wish were §programmatically-introspectable; §all-alternatives-reported-no-heuristic borrowable for any §multi-candidate-failure surface (slot machine combinator failures, predicate disjunctions; §Rust-compiler-error-analogy is citable prior art); §two-consumer-postures-named-explicitly with §default-favors-the-tighter-budget-consumer borrowable for any §dual-audience API where humans and AI agents both consume output; Kris Kowal (prompted by maintainer kriskowal) authored)

### From endo packages/{trampoline,memoize,nat}/{src,docs,README.md} (Cycle 199 chat-lane; 232 source + 469 docs; §thirty-third consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-199; §eighteenth-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family; **single most structurally interesting move**: §three-tight-utilities sharing §a-common-dependency-on-harden-or-freeze-substitute but otherwise §no-internal-dependencies (sibling-pattern to cycle 195 cli/src cluster) + §each-utility-has-its-own-load-bearing-structural-move; **@endo/trampoline (59 lines)**: §classic-uncurry-this-via-bind.bind(bind.call) — Mark Miller's canonical idiom for prototype-tamper-resistant method capture: `bind.bind(bind.call)` returns `f` such that `f(method)(receiver, ...args) === method.call(receiver, ...args)`; §capture-the-prototype-not-the-instance — `generatorPrototype.next`/`.throw` captured once at module load; §rationale: iterator instances may have their `.next` overridden per-instance, but the prototype's original is what the uncurry locks in; §sync/async-two-color-sharing-via-generator: same generator function body driven §synchronously (yielded values used directly) or §asynchronously (yielded values awaited); §the-author-writes-one-generator-function-and-picks-the-trampoline-at-the-call-site; sibling to Haskell IO monad and Rust async-trait — §abstract-the-effect-color; §generator-throw-send-error-into-generator: when `generatorNext` throws (or async `await` throws), `generatorThrow(iterator, err)` resumes the generator §at-the-`yield`-with-`throw` rather than `next`; §the-generator-can-catch-the-error-in-a-try/catch-around-the-yield and continue; §without-this the trampoline would have no way to send errors back into the generator's control flow; §two-eslint-discipline-aware-exceptions named with file-local comments: `@endo/no-polymorphic-call` on the uncurry-this line + `@jessie.js/safe-await-separator` at file head; §both-rules-are-discipline-aware-exceptions where the package's specific need overrides the general rule with linter exception local to the file; §types.d.ts: `TrampolineResult<TFn>` extracts `Generator<any, infer TResult>` return type; `SyncTrampolineResult<TFn>` further narrows by rejecting if result is Promise — §type-system-encodes-the-color-asymmetry; **@endo/memoize (54 lines source + 76 README + 176 docs/memoize.md)**: §encapsulated-pumpkin-sentinel — `const encapsulatedPumpkin = harden({})` marked "must not escape this module" — §the-honor-system-discipline (module's authors commit to never returning it from any export); §why-`harden({})`-not-`Symbol()`: hardened empty object is §a-valid-WeakMap-key whereas primitive Symbol may not be; §local-sentinel-avoids-Eval-Twin issues (registered symbols are twin-safe but local symbols are not); §one-sentinel-three-purposes: (1) §recursion-protection (`if (memoedResult === encapsulatedPumpkin) throw new TypeError('no recursion through memoization with same arg')`); (2) §non-weak-key-compat-early-error (`memo.set(arg, encapsulatedPumpkin)` throws synchronously if arg isn't a valid WeakMap key — error raised before `fn` is called, user sees WeakMap error not confusingly-late mismatch); (3) §try/catch-cleanup-on-fn-throw (`try { result = fn(arg); } catch (e) { memo.delete(arg); throw e; }` — recursion-protection marker removed so a later call can retry); §code-density-with-named-effect; §each-purpose-would-otherwise-require-its-own-check; §throws-from-`fn(arg)`-are-not-memoized; §rejected-promises-returned-by-`fn(arg)`-***are***-memoized (docs make this explicit); §four-tier-safety-hierarchy in docs/memoize.md: (1) Base-semantics — Hardened-JS-substrate must hold (WeakMap conforms to spec); §`lockdown()` makes this non-violable post-lockdown; (2) Defensiveness — `fn` must throw on invalid WeakMap keys and non-memoization candidates; (3) Unobservable-Memoization — three named requirements (fn transitively-immutable-and-powerless / fn(arg) must not cause effects on success — proxy-arg makes this hard / fn(arg) deterministic for same arg); (4) Preserving-Isolation (not-a-communications-channel) — four named requirements including §determinism-with-fresh-identity-allowance (result objects: transitively-immutable-and-powerless + equivalent-aside-from-object-identity + either-always-same-identity-or-fresh-allocation-per-call; §the-weaker-determinism-allows-always-fresh-but-otherwise-equivalent); §the-hierarchy-is-monotonic — each tier strengthens the previous; §contingent-safety framing because §the-tooling-cannot-check-or-enforce-the-requirements; §passStyleOf-cited-as-canonical-memoize-user: case-splits and only memoizes its internal algorithm for the WeakMap-key cases; §passStyleOf-itself-is-not-the-memoFn but rather a wrapper that delegates the WeakMap-key case to an internally-memoized helper; §Eval-Twin-Problem reference to [endojs/endo#1583](https://github.com/endojs/endo/issues/1583) — joining cycle 197 panic's chain; §the-memoize-doc names the §haphazard-duplication-must-be-unobservable design discipline; §`harden(memoize)` after `harden(memoFn)` — both the factory and the products are hardened; **@endo/nat (119 lines source + 116 README)**: §Apps-Script-bigint-literal-workaround — `ZERO_N = BigInt(0)` and `ONE_N = BigInt(1)` not `0n`/`1n` because Apps Script's parser doesn't support BigInt literal syntax (causes syntax error); the comment-block six paragraphs explains §"actually-when-a-number-is-accurate-we-can-use-that-instead-of-a-string" honest-narrowing — Apps Script wants strings but `0`/`1` are exact JS numbers so cleaner `BigInt(0)` form is used; §explicit-narrowing disclaimer "Endo is not in general trying for compat with Apps Script. But packages that will have minimal dependencies after adapting to [PR #3008] might, such as `@endo/marshal` and `@endo/ocapn`" — prevents future readers from thinking Endo targets Apps Script; §the-narrow-targeting-is-explicit: minimal-dependency packages might care, rest of Endo does not; §comment-block-duplicated-verbatim above ZERO_N and ONE_N — each export stands alone with its own justification; §the-comment-is-six-paragraphs-of-prose explaining §a-two-line-export; §freeze-as-harden-substitute-pending-PR-#3008 with §explicit-honest-deferral and §named-equivalence-rationale: "freeze in this case is actually equivalent to harden" because "we're only using it on unadorned arrow functions" — §arrow-functions-have-no-prototype-property (no chain to harden); §the-comment-prevents-a-future-reader-from-changing-this-without-understanding-why-it's-safe; §sibling to cycle 197 panic's §Object.freeze discipline; §the-PR-#3008-reference is §named-future-state; §`isNat` + `Nat` predicate-assertion-pair: isNat returns boolean; Nat returns bigint or throws; §three-distinct-error-conditions: (1) Bigint-negative → RangeError "is negative"; (2) Number-not-safe-integer → RangeError "is not a safe integer"; (3) Number-negative → RangeError "is negative"; (4) Neither-bigint-nor-number → TypeError "is a ${typeof} but must be a bigint or a number"; §two-different-error-types-encode-two-different-failure-categories: TypeError = wrong-kind-of-value (caller passed string/object/undefined — a bug in the caller); RangeError = right-kind-wrong-value (number/bigint failed value-constraint — a runtime value out of domain); §coerce-to-bigint-on-success — `Nat(5)` returns `5n`; consumers can rely on output type being bigint; §README explanation of "safely representable" with §IEEE-754-discipline — `2**70+1 === 2**70` because outside contiguous range; safe range `-(2**53-1)` to `2**53-1`; safe natural numbers `0` to `2**53-1`; §bigint-is-inherently-safe (every bigint `>= 0n` safely represents a natural number); §"A-skippable-detail" tag as §explicit-named-skip-marker for §progressive-disclosure-in-prose; §cross-cutting patterns: all three target `@endo/marshal` and/or `@endo/ocapn` minimal-dependency aspiration; §three-different-approaches-to-the-same-harden-discipline (trampoline doesn't harden — caller's responsibility; memoize fully hardens — most defensive; nat uses freeze-as-substitute pending PR #3008 — middle ground); §minimal-dependency-discipline is the shared constraint enforced by the marshal aspiration; §implicitly-tested-by-the-larger-consumer sibling to cycle 195 cli/src cluster; §tier-1 borrowing: §classic-uncurry-this-via-bind.bind(bind.call) + §capture-the-prototype-not-the-instance + §sync/async-two-color-sharing-via-generator-trampoline + §generator-throw-send-error-into-generator + §eslint-discipline-aware-exceptions-with-file-local-comment + §encapsulated-pumpkin-sentinel + §one-sentinel-three-purposes + §four-tier-safety-hierarchy + §contingent-safety-framing + §throws-not-memoized-vs-rejected-promises-memoized + §passStyleOf-as-cited-flagship-consumer + §Apps-Script-bigint-literal-workaround + §explicit-narrowing + §freeze-as-harden-substitute-with-named-equivalence-rationale + §two-different-error-types + §predicate-assertion-pair + §coerce-to-bigint-on-success + §safely-representable-IEEE-754-integer-discipline + §skippable-detail-tag + §comment-block-duplicated-verbatim-above-sibling-exports + §harden-the-factory-and-the-products; §synthesis-target: slot machine library can §borrow-the-trampoline-pattern for sync/async-shared-deck-shuffling-algorithm — same algorithm body, two effect colors; §uncurry-this idiom borrowable for any §pre-lockdown-utility that needs §prototype-tamper-resistance; §encapsulated-pumpkin-sentinel borrowable for any §self-referential-WeakMap-recursion-protection; §one-sentinel-three-purposes shape is §a-template-for-tight-utility-code; §four-tier-safety-hierarchy borrowable as §documentation-shape for security-sensitive-utility with multiple property-levels; §contingent-safety-framing is §the-honest-shape when the tooling cannot check the requirements; §Apps-Script-bigint-literal-workaround borrowable where §minimal-dependency-package-discipline meets §exotic-runtime-target-constraints; §two-different-error-types pattern borrowable for §any-validation-function distinguishing §wrong-kind from §right-kind-wrong-value; §predicate-assertion-pair borrowable wherever §the-caller-might-want-either-branching-or-asserting; §coerce-to-bigint-on-success borrowable for §type-uniformity-at-the-output; §the-Eval-Twin-Problem-is-load-bearing-across-the-@endo-substrate; Mark Miller + Kris Kowal + Endo contributors authored)

### From endo-but-for-bots designs/worker-rust-xs.md (Cycle 200 designs-lane milestone; 540-line Status **Not Started** design by Kris Kowal (prompted) 2026-03-23; **the foundational predecessor design for the XS-worker family** that cycles 176/178/182/184/188 all build on; §thirty-fourth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-200; **single most structurally interesting move**: §engine-level-confinement-via-XS-native-Compartment-vs-SES-shim-source-rewriting (V8 has no native Compartment; the shim emulates confinement by rewriting source text but cannot enforce module-level isolation at the engine level; XS implements Compartment + ModuleSource natively) + §host-compartment-vs-guest-compartment-split-with-cap-std-backed-powers + §three-numbered-problems-each-with-named-defense + §in-process-host-functions-not-IPC + §Known-Gaps-instead-of-Open-Questions + §Prompt-section-preserves-discard-prior-design-narrative; §the-decisive-engine-choice: §for-confined-workers-running-capability-mediated-code, §engine-speed-matters-less-than-confinement-correctness — V8's JIT and DevTools tradedown to XS's native Compartment because §the-shim-is-a-compatibility-layer-not-a-boundary; §Prompt-section preserves maintainer's "I would like to discard this design and try another approach" verbatim with three arguments named: (1) supervisor-as-thin-backbone-is-correct (this design's chosen shape); (2) supervisor-responsible-for-all-I/O-is-a-bottleneck (rejected the earlier sketch); (3) choose-XS-simply-because-of-Compartment-support — §to-hide-host-APIs-from-the-guest-program; §honest-design-evolution-in-the-Prompt-section sibling to cycles 178/180/183/184/188/192/196/197/198/200 §honest-design-evolution-record family; §three-numbered-problems: (1) SES-shim-is-compatibility-layer-not-boundary (V8 cannot enforce engine-level isolation); (2) every-I/O-operation-crosses-a-process-boundary (workers communicate via CapTP over pipes; supervisor becomes bottleneck); (3) (third problem; see source); §each-problem-has-named-defense: Problem 1 → XS-native-Compartment (engine-level enforcement vs source-rewriting); Problem 2 → in-process-host-functions-not-IPC + cap-std-backed-powers inside worker; Problem 3 → worker-process-not-supervisor-process; §symmetric-problem/defense-enumeration sibling-pattern to cycle 196 endoclaw's §three-named-attacks-with-three-structural-defenses and cycle 200 hardened-url-shim's §two-specific-hazards-with-named-defense-each; §ASCII-architecture-diagram with three-process-boxes (supervisor + daemon + Rust/XS worker) and §nested-compartments-inside-worker (host containing guest); §host-compartment receives cap-std-backed endowments (FilePowers → cap-std::fs::Dir handles; CryptoPowers → sha2 + ed25519-dalek + rand; NetworkPowers → cap-net-ext::Pool; console → stderr write; TextEncoder/TextDecoder/URL; E/Far/makeExo/M from @endo/captp + @endo/exo + @endo/patterns); §guest-compartment receives only daemon-explicit endowments — no FilePowers/CryptoPowers/NetworkPowers; §the-guest-has-no-host-API-access; §it-can-only-reach-I/O-through-capabilities-passed-via-CapTP (the `powers` argument to `make(powers, context)`); §same-trust-architecture-as-today's-SES-based-workers, but §enforced-by-the-engine-rather-than-by-source-rewriting; §cap-std for capability-based I/O at syscall level: §the-Rust-worker-opens-`Dir`-handles-at-startup-and-provides-them-to-JS-as-directory-tokens; §cap-std-rejects-path-traversal-and-symlink-escapes-at-the-syscall-level via `openat(2)` / `renameat(2)` with dir-fd as base; §the-kernel-does-the-confinement, §independent-of-any-JS-level-checks; §defense-in-depth at kernel level; §three-different-layers-of-capability-based-FS-confinement implemented at §three-different-levels (cycle 166 daemon-mount JS-level / this design Rust syscall-level / cycle 190 endo-posix-sandbox Rust process-level); §in-process-host-functions-not-IPC: §XS-host-functions-are-direct-C-function-calls-from-JS; no serialization, no pipe I/O, no nonce correlation; §for-a-`readFile`-call, the path goes from JS-string → C-string → cap-std → C-string → JS-string, all within one process; §orders-of-magnitude-faster than envelope-based RPC for high-frequency I/O; §the-rejection-of-envelope-RPC-for-host-functions is §a-named-architectural-trade-off; §when-trust-boundary-is-not-the-process-boundary, §use-direct-FFI-not-RPC; §worker-process-not-supervisor-process: supervisor stays thin (routing only); eliminates IPC overhead; each worker has its own cap-std handles scoped to its directories; §heterogeneous-workers: §Node.js-for-development (Chrome DevTools) + §Rust/XS-for-deployment (engine-level confinement); §the-supervisor-is-platform-agnostic at envelope layer; §sibling-to cycle 176 endor-architecture's §three-worker-platforms-with-byte-identical-CBOR-envelopes — §this-design-is-the-precursor with §two-worker-platforms; §cycle-176-extends-it-to-three; §pre-compiled-bytecode-for-Endo-modules at build time eliminates parse overhead at worker startup; source positions preserved in debug builds for `xsbug`; §sibling-to cycle 175 harden-selector's §pin-on-first-install (both §front-load-the-cost-to-amortize-runtime); §eight-Design-Decisions canonical format: XS-over-V8 + worker-process-not-supervisor + in-process-host-functions-not-IPC + cap-std-for-capability-based-I/O + host-compartment-/-guest-compartment-split + pre-compiled-bytecode-for-Endo-modules + Node.js-workers-remain-for-development + SharedArrayBuffer-deferred; §each-decision-names-the-alternative-rejected with rationale; §Decision-8-SharedArrayBuffer-deferred is §future-named-with-condition ("until profiling shows copy overhead matters"); §six-Implementation-Phases with L/M/S effort sizing: Phase 1 (XS Embedding Crate, L) + Phase 2 (Host Powers, M) + Phase 3 (Endo Module Loading, L) + Phase 4 (CapTP and Envelope Integration, M) + Phase 5 (Worker Bootstrap and Integration, M) + Phase 6 (Platform Adapter, S); §L-rated-phases (1+3) are foundation building; §M-rated-phases (2/4/5) are integration and port; §S-rated phase 6 is the platform adapter; §sibling-to cycle 196 endoclaw's §gap-priority-classification (High/Medium/Low) — both name priority-axis but for different things: 196 for gaps-to-close; 200-worker-rust-xs for effort-of-phases; §Known-Gaps-instead-of-Open-Questions: §five-empirical-verification-items in checkbox-task-list format `- [ ]`: (1) XS C API stability — verify Compartment and ModuleSource APIs are stable across XS releases; (2) @endo/captp on XS — verify CapTP's full protocol (including HandledPromise) works under the `xs` condition; (3) `xsbug` TCP adapter — evaluate effort to connect xsbug to the running worker for remote debugging; (4) XS memory model — understand XS's garbage collector behavior under sustained worker loads; (5) Async I/O model — XS has different event loop model than Node.js; host functions that perform async I/O need a Tokio ↔ XS promise bridge; §Known-Gaps-as-checklist when waiting on empirical-verification rather than decisional-questions; §sibling-pattern: cycles 196/198/200-hardened-url-shim with §Open-Questions; cycle 200-worker-rust-xs with §Known-Gaps — both valid shapes for named-uncertainty but at different abstraction layers (§questions-are-decisional; §gaps-are-empirical); §borrowable-pattern: §Known-Gaps-as-checklist for §pre-implementation-investigation-items; §the-XS-worker-family-foundational-predecessor: cycle 176 (endor-architecture) carries this design's worker into the larger Rust supervisor architecture; cycle 178 (xs-worker-snapshot) depends on this design's worker shape; cycle 182 (xs-worker-debugger) is the §`xsbug` integration named in Known Gap #3; cycle 184 (xs-worker-metering) integrates with this worker; cycle 188 (rust-xs-performance) is empirical follow-up to Known Gap #4 (XS memory model); §the-§Known-Gaps-section-is-a-roadmap-for-future-designs (5 gaps spawn at least 3 named sibling designs); §the-supplant-pattern: §worker-rust-xs (cycle 200, Not Started) → §daemon-endor-architecture (cycle 176, Active) — §this-design's-supervisor-slot is §inter-worker-routing-only; ### From endo packages/immutable-arraybuffer/{src/immutable-arraybuffer-pony.js,src/immutable-arraybuffer-shim.js,README.md} (Cycle 201 chat-lane; 350 source (253 ponyfill + 97 shim) + 75 README; §thirty-fifth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-201; §nineteenth-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family; **single most structurally interesting move**: §Ponyfill-plus-Shim full-version (sibling to cycle 197 panic's ponyfill-only deferred-shim) + §Purposeful-Violation section explicitly named in README + §WeakMap-as-emulated-private-field-AND-brand-check (Hermes-no-class-private-fields) + §intermediate-prototype-`ImmutableArrayBufferInternalPrototype`-inheriting-from-ArrayBuffer.prototype + §three-tier-fallback-with-three-platform-degradation + §two-named-motivations-from-orthogonal-domains; §Ponyfill (index.js) defines and exports new things without modifying old (`transferBufferToImmutable`/`sliceBufferToImmutable`/`isBufferImmutable`); §Shim (shim.js) modifies existing JavaScript primordials by adding `transferToImmutable`/`sliceToImmutable`/`immutable` to ArrayBuffer.prototype; §two-named-policies-for-the-shim: §modern-shim-practice-frowns-on-conditional-installation (so changes to the proposal don't break old code depending on old shim) — warn-and-continue if detecting prior installation; §shim-still-runs-after-native-implementation (will require a later manual step to delete the shim, after manual analysis of the compat implications); §Purposeful-Violation section: `Symbol.toStringTag = 'ImmutableArrayBuffer'` (not 'ArrayBuffer') reduces fidelity but defeats concordance's `toString()` sniff (Ava's diagnostic library; concordance would otherwise assume the object is a genuine ArrayBuffer and try `Buffer.from`); §the-pattern: when-you-must-violate-fidelity-name-the-violation-and-its-rationale; §six+-named-Caveats in README: (1) platform-degradation (Hermes, Node ≤16, some JavaScriptCore — neither structuredClone nor ArrayBuffer.prototype.transfer); (2) no-intermediate-prototype-in-proposal; (3) intermediate-prototype-discoverable-not-encapsulated; (4) not-real-exotic-objects (cannot be DataView/TypedArray backing stores); (5) cannot-be-cloned-or-transferred-between-JS-threads; (6) shim-still-runs-after-native-implementation; (7) not-hardened-by-itself — the ses-shim is expected to import and treat as additional primordials to be hardened during lockdown's harden phase; §WeakMap-as-emulated-private-field with explicit honest acknowledgment "If we could use classes with private fields everywhere, this would have been a `this.#buffer` private field on an `ImmutableArrayBufferInternal` class. But we cannot do so on Hermes" — §the-WeakMap-emulates-the-private-field-AND-the-brand-check; §three-different-ways-to-emulate-private-state in JavaScript across @endo: §pumpkin-sentinel (cycle 199 memoize) + §registered-symbol (cycle 197 panic) + §WeakMap (this cycle); §the-WeakMap-approach-is-the-only-one-that-also-serves-as-brand-check by-construction; §method-binding-pre-defineProperty on the WeakMap (`buffers.has = buffers.has` etc) to avoid post-hoc prototype lookups (defense against prototype tampering); §intermediate-prototype `ImmutableArrayBufferInternalPrototype.__proto__ = arrayBufferPrototype` — §emulated-immutable-buffers-inherit-from-ArrayBuffer.prototype-transitively so `x instanceof ArrayBuffer` acts as proposed; §five-throw-methods on intermediate prototype (resize / transfer / transferToFixedLength / transferToImmutable + slice stays mutable but sliceToImmutable returns new immutable) + §six-getter-overrides (byteLength / detached false / maxByteLength / resizable false / immutable true / toStringTag); §two-distinct-throw-messages — "Cannot resize an immutable ArrayBuffer" for resize; "Cannot detach an immutable ArrayBuffer" for transfer/transferToFixedLength/transferToImmutable — §two-error-categories with named-rationale; §brand-check-via-`getBuffer(this)` on every accessor — even getters that don't need the buffer-value call it for the side-effect (the throw on non-emulated instances); §slice-stays-mutable-vs-sliceToImmutable distinguishes mutable-copy from new-immutable; §three-tier-fallback for transfer mechanism: `ArrayBuffer.prototype.transfer` (Node 21+) → `structuredClone({transfer: [...]})` (Node 17+) → undefined (graceful degradation; ponyfill+shim fail to initialize on known-deficient platforms); §zero-length-slice-as-genuine-ArrayBuffer-enforcement — `arrayBufferSlice(arrayBuffer, 0, 0)` before structuredClone (the side-effect is enforcing arrayBuffer is a genuine exotic object; comment "Hopefully, a zero-length slice is cheap, but still enforces that arrayBuffer is a genuine ArrayBuffer exotic object" — §honest-comment-naming-cost-uncertainty); §capture-before-scuttled pre-lockdown discipline — eight intrinsics captured at module load (ArrayBuffer / Object / Reflect / Symbol / TypeError / Uint8Array / WeakMap / structuredClone) with named comment "Capture structuredClone before it can be scuttled"; §belt-and-suspenders-freeze on must-not-escape factory `makeImmutableArrayBufferInternal` — comment names that it must not escape (module exports only high-level functions, not the internal factory); freeze provides defense-in-depth; §warning-not-error-when-overwriting prior installation: §"Modern shim practice frowns on conditional installation, at least for proposals prior to stage 3. This is so changes to the proposal since an old shim was distributed don't need to worry about the proposal breaking old code depending on the old shim. Thus, if we detect that we're about to overwrite a prior installation, we simply issue this warning and continue"; §TODO-comment naming the known-broken-interaction with lockdown's frozen primordials ("if the primordials are frozen after the prior implementation, such as by lockdown, then this precludes overwriting as expected. However, for this case, the following warning text will be confusing"); §encapsulated-genuine-ArrayBuffer-with-exclusive-access — security demands exclusive access; immutability enforced by never modifying it; §two-named-motivations from orthogonal domains: (1) §ROM-vs-RAM Moddable XS rationale — "Some JavaScript implementations, like Moddable XS, bring JavaScript to embedded systems, like device controllers, where ROM is much more plentiful and cheaper than RAM. These systems need to place voluminous fixed data into ROM, and currently do so using semantics outside the official JavaScript standard"; (2) §By-copy network protocol rationale — "The OCapN network protocol treats strings and byte-arrays as distinct forms of bulk data to be transmitted by copy. At JavaScript endpoints speaking OCapN such as `@endo/pass-style` + `@endo/marshal`, JavaScript strings represent OCapN strings. The immutability of strings in the JavaScript language reflects their by-copy nature in the protocol. Likewise, to reflect an OCapN byte-array well into the JavaScript language, we need an immutable container of bulk binary data"; §two-motivations-from-orthogonal-domains converging on the same primitive as §the-shape-of-a-proposal-rationale; §plain-JavaScript-not-Hardened-JavaScript disclaimer — "Thus, the objects and function it creates are not hardened by this ponyfill/shim itself. Rather, the ses-shim is expected to import these, and then treat the resulting objects as if they were additional primordials, to be hardened during lockdown's harden phase"; §the-Eval-Twin-Problem-chain extends implicitly to this package; §tier-1 borrowing: §Ponyfill+Shim-pattern-full-version + §Purposeful-Violation-section-in-README + §concordance-sniff-defense-via-Symbol.toStringTag + §six+-named-Caveats-as-honest-enumeration-of-limitations + §WeakMap-as-emulated-private-field-AND-brand-check + §method-binding-pre-defineProperty + §intermediate-prototype-inheriting-from-the-host-prototype + §five-throw-methods-on-immutable-prototype-with-two-distinct-error-messages + §slice-stays-mutable-vs-sliceToImmutable-distinction + §brand-check-via-`getBuffer(this)`-on-every-accessor + §three-tier-fallback-with-named-known-deficient-platforms + §zero-length-slice-as-genuine-ArrayBuffer-enforcement + §capture-before-scuttled + §belt-and-suspenders-freeze-on-must-not-escape-factory + §modern-shim-practice-frowns-on-conditional-installation + §shim-still-runs-after-native-implementation + §warning-not-error-when-overwriting-with-TODO-naming-broken-lockdown-interaction + §two-named-motivations-from-orthogonal-domains + §plain-JavaScript-not-Hardened-JavaScript-disclaimer + §encapsulated-genuine-ArrayBuffer-with-exclusive-access; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §immutable-game-state-shapes can §borrow-the-WeakMap-as-emulated-private-field-AND-brand-check directly (game-state object whose internal fields are private via WeakMap with brand-check for "only-our-emulated-states-pass-this-check"); §Ponyfill+Shim-pattern borrowable for any §new-method-on-existing-prototype where callers might prefer either shape; §Purposeful-Violation-section borrowable as a README shape for §named-deliberate-deviations from fidelity discoverable by future maintainers; §three-tier-fallback borrowable for any §feature-that-requires-platform-support with §known-deficient-platforms-named-explicitly; §two-named-motivations-from-orthogonal-domains converging on the same primitive borrowable as §the-shape-of-a-proposal-rationale; §concordance-sniff-defense borrowable wherever §a-diagnostic-library-uses-pattern-matching that the emulation needs to defeat; §capture-before-scuttled borrowable for any §pre-lockdown-utility that needs §prototype-tamper-resistance; Mark Miller + Kris Kowal + Endo contributors authored)

### From endo-but-for-bots designs/endor-run-expanded.md (Cycle 202 designs-lane; 359-line Status **In Progress** design by Kris Kowal (prompted) 2026-04-17; Phases 1-2 shipped, Phases 3-5 remaining; §thirty-sixth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-202; **single most structurally interesting move**: §three-input-forms-converging-on-one-runtime-path (ZIP archive / unpacked directory / entry-point module — all flow into §CAS-backed-module-loading) + §input-form-detection-by-magic-bytes-not-flags (PK\x03\x04 ZIP magic; compartment-map.json presence; fallthrough to entry-point) + §three-option-mapper-implementation with §shell-out-to-Node-rejected ("defeats the purpose of `endor run` being self-contained") + §XS-hosted-mapper-reusing-battle-tested-@endo/compartment-mapper + §lazy-module-loading-from-CAS-by-hash-on-demand; §The-design's-central-axis: §all-three-input-forms-converge-on-the-same-CAS-backed-module-loading-runtime-path; §the-only-difference-between-forms-is-the-ingestion-phase; §CAS-as-universal-backing-store (Design Decision 1); §three-input-forms: Form 1 ZIP archive (PK\x03\x04 magic or .zip extension); Form 2 unpacked directory (compartment-map.json presence); Form 3 entry-point module (fallthrough — single .js file or package.json); §the-CLI-detects-the-form-by-examining-the-path with §explicit-flags-available-for-disambiguation-but-rarely-needed (`--zip`, `--dir`, `--entry`); §Design-Decision-5: "Input form detection by file type, not flags. `endor run foo` examines `foo` to determine the form"; §three-option-mapper-implementation: Option A Rust-native (preferred long-term — needs JS parser for import/require extraction + package.json reading + registry table); Option B XS-hosted (CHOSEN near-term — reuses battle-tested @endo/compartment-mapper code by running it inside an XS machine with fs powers; "~100ms startup overhead for the mapper machine — acceptable for a CLI tool"); Option C shell-out-to-Node (REJECTED — "defeats the purpose of `endor run` being self-contained"); §reuse-battle-tested-code-via-running-it-inside-the-target-engine borrowable for any §code-reuse-without-port; §self-contained-binary-as-design-axiom — sibling to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs's §XS-over-V8 (both designs reject Node.js dependencies in deployment target); §two-phase-flow for Form 3: Phase-A §map-in-XS-with-fs-powers + ingest sources to CAS; Phase-B §execute-in-fresh-XS-with-CAS-backed-loading-only; §two-machines-not-one — §two-machines-of-the-same-engine for §two-different-capability-scopes (mapping has fs powers; execution has CAS-backed-loading-only); §sibling-pattern to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs's §host-compartment-vs-guest-compartment-split (same architectural discipline at different layers — build vs runtime); §CAS-backed-module-loading: `load_archive_from_cas(cas, root_hash) → LoadedArchive`; §the-runtime-behavior-is-identical-regardless-of-input-form; §the-import-hook-fetches-module-bytes-lazily-from-the-CAS-on-first-import; §lazy-module-loading is important for §large-applications where §only-a-subset-of-modules-are-actually-imported-at-runtime; §root-hash-printed-to-stderr: `endor[run]: archive root sha256:abc123...` so subsequent `endor run --cas sha256:abc123...` skips re-ingestion entirely; §expensive-ingestion-with-cheap-retrieval; §the-hash-becomes-the-handle; §sibling-pattern to cycle 175 harden-selector's §pin-on-first-install (pinned name as stable handle), cycle 199 trampoline's §classic-uncurry-this (early capture as stable handle); §--cas-dir-flag for non-default CAS location — useful for §standalone-runs-without-a-running-daemon, isolated testing, scratch caches; §standalone-CAS-when-no-daemon (Design Decision 4): `endor run` creates local CAS in temp directory; doesn't couple to daemon lifecycle; benefits from daemon's CAS at `{statePath}/store-sha256` when present; §the-CLI-doesn't-require-the-daemon but §benefits-from-it-when-present; §--no-cas-fallback-for-read-only-filesystems — backward-compatibility-via-flag with named use case; §the-default-is-the-new-CAS-backed-behavior; §opt-out-restores-legacy; §sibling-pattern to cycle 197 panic's §default-erroneous-exit + no-ambient-normal-exit asymmetry — both designs preserve legacy behavior via explicit opt-out flag; §Status-section-with-completed-phases-and-code-paths-named ("Phase 2: `rust/endo/src/cas_archive.rs` — `ingest_archive` extracts ZIP contents into CAS as blobs with tree manifests. `load_archive_from_cas` reconstructs `LoadedArchive` from a root hash. `endor run` now ingests to CAS and prints root hash. `endor run --cas <hash>` re-runs from CAS. `--no-cas` preserves legacy behavior. `run_xs_archive_loaded` added to xsnap for executing pre-loaded archives") — §code-paths-named-as-greppable-references for §future-readers; §sibling-pattern to cycle 188 daemon-rust-xs-performance's §Working-copy-inventory section; §five-Design-Decisions canonical format: (1) CAS-as-the-universal-backing-store; (2) Lazy-module-loading-from-CAS; (3) XS-hosted-compartment-mapper near-term; (4) Standalone-CAS-for-`endor run`; (5) Input-form-detection-by-file-type-not-flags; §five-Implementation-Phases each with named test cases per phase as §greppable-completion-criteria: Phase 1 ContentStore standalone (store/fetch round-trip), Phase 2 ZIP CAS ingestion (verify CAS files + re-run from hash), Phase 3 directory input (compartment-map.json + sources, execute), Phase 4 entry-point no-deps (bundle mapper for XS + two-phase flow + simple module), Phase 5 entry-point with deps (package.json resolution in XS-hosted mapper + node_modules walk + registry lookup); §three-dependencies-with-named-relationship-types: §Requires daemon-cas-management (ContentStore for blob/tree storage, retain/release); §Enables endor-npm-registry-proxy (Form 3 package resolution without node_modules); §Extends daemon-endor-architecture (`endor run` becomes CAS-aware); §three-relationship-types-as-Dependencies-table-shape borrowable for §multi-design-context with §named-coupling-strengths; §four-CLI-options with named-defaults symmetric across §three-input-shapes (hash via --cas / directory via path / file via path); §honest-cost-disclosure ("~100ms startup overhead for the mapper machine — acceptable for a CLI tool") with §form-factor-context for the acceptable trade-off; §tier-1 borrowing: §three-input-forms-converging-on-one-runtime-path + §input-form-detection-by-magic-bytes-not-flags + §three-option-implementation-with-rejected-option-named + §reuse-battle-tested-code-via-running-it-inside-the-target-engine + §two-phase-flow with §two-machines-of-the-same-engine for §two-different-capability-scopes + §lazy-module-loading-from-CAS-by-hash-on-demand + §root-hash-printed-to-stderr-for-re-run + §standalone-mode-when-no-daemon + §backward-compatibility-via-flag with named use case + §Status-section-with-completed-phases-and-code-paths-named + §five-Design-Decisions canonical format + §five-Implementation-Phases with named test cases per phase + §three-dependencies-with-named-relationship-types + §four-CLI-options with named defaults + §self-contained-binary as named design axiom + §honest-cost-disclosure with form-factor-context; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §multi-input-game-loader can accept §pre-bundled-game-archive + §unpacked-game-directory + §entry-point-JS-file converging on §sandboxed-evaluation runtime path; §root-hash-printed-to-stderr borrowable for §reproducible-game-replay (same hash + same RNG seed = same outcome); §lazy-module-loading borrowable for §large-game-asset-libraries; §standalone-mode borrowable for §desktop-game-installations; §reuse-via-running-inside-target-engine borrowable for §game-asset-bundler-inside-game-engine's-JS-runtime; Kris Kowal (prompted) authored)

### From endo packages/cache-map/{src/cachemap.js,README.md} (Cycle 203 chat-lane; 317 source + 54 README; §thirty-seventh consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-203; §twentieth-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family; **single most structurally interesting move**: §Bounded-size-cache-with-WeakMap-compatible-interface (`has`/`get`/`set`/`delete`) + §`makeMap`-option-as-key-strategy (controls §key-validity + §comparison + §referential-strength simultaneously; default WeakMap for weak; Map for strong arbitrary) + §doubly-linked-ring-with-sentinel-head (head.next=head; head.prev=head initially; §sentinel's-`data.has`-throws "internal: sentinel head cell has no data" to catch accidental use) + §touch-moves-to-first-LRU-evicts-last + §LRU-or-better-eviction-with-CLOCK-and-SIEVE-as-named-alternatives + §each-cell-holds-a-SingleEntryMap with §three-strategy-cascade for reset (delete known key / clear if supported / replace entirely for WeakMap path); §The-`makeMap`-option-defaults-to-WeakMap but accepts §any-producer-of-objects-with-WeakMap-interface; §Map-allows-arbitrary-keys-which-will-be-strongly-held; §one-option-three-axes simultaneously; §try-as-factory-fall-back-to-constructor (`MaybeCtor()` then `new MaybeCtor()`) for §APIs-that-accept-both-shapes — WeakMap and Map both throw when called without `new` so they take the second path; custom factories take the first; §sentinel-head with §three-cell-operations: appendNewCell (splice after prev), moveCellAfter (no-op if already in position; otherwise splice out + splice in), resetCell (preserve Map via delete(oldKey) when possible; use clear() if available; else replace entirely for WeakMap path); §UNKNOWN_KEY-sentinel-symbol — `const UNKNOWN_KEY = Symbol('UNKNOWN_KEY')` local-symbol (not registered) marking §"caller-doesn't-have-the-key" to `resetCell`; §four-different-sentinel-shapes in @endo now: §local-symbol (cycle 203 cache-map's UNKNOWN_KEY) + §registered-symbol (cycle 197 panic's PanicEndowmentSymbol = Symbol.for(...)) + §pumpkin-sentinel (cycle 199 memoize's `harden({})`) + §WeakMap (cycle 201 immutable-arraybuffer's `buffers`); §each-sentinel-shape has §a-different-cross-cutting-need that motivates its specific design; §touch-moves-to-first: every cache operation flows through `touchKey` which moves the cell to first position; §the-LRU-victim-is-then-`head.prev`; §every-cache-operation-touches: has/get/set all flow through touchKey; §even-a-miss-counts-toward-totalQueryCount; §insertion-flow on miss: if cellCount<capacity then appendNewCell at first + increment cellCount after successful creation; else reuse the tail (head.prev) + resetCell + moveCellAfter; §don't-establish-entry-until-prior-steps-succeed — `if (cell) keyToCell.set(key, cell)` last so failures leave no half-state (sibling to cycle 199 memoize's §try/catch-cleanup-on-fn-throw); §LRU-or-better-eviction with §named-alternatives-with-citations (CLOCK from Wikipedia + SIEVE from sievecache.com); §implementation-flexibility-via-bounded-quality-promise — current implementation is LRU via doubly-linked ring; could swap to CLOCK or SIEVE without API change; §sibling-pattern to cycle 199 memoize's §contingent-safety framing (both designs §promise-bounded-behavior without committing to a specific algorithm); §each-cell-holds-a-SingleEntryMap typedef (WeakMapAPI<K,V> & ({clear?: undefined} | Pick<Map<K,V>, 'clear'>)) — a WeakMap or Map holding exactly one entry; §three-reset-strategies enumerated in resetCell: (1) delete the known old key; (2) clear() if supported (Map but not WeakMap); (3) replace the Map entirely (WeakMap path); §WeakMap-instances-must-be-replaced-when-key-unknown — §information-theoretic-limit: §you-cannot-iterate-a-WeakMap-to-clear-it (by design — WeakMaps don't expose iteration); §the-only-way-to-clear-a-WeakMap-without-knowing-its-keys is §to-discard-it-and-make-a-new-one; §"delete" is a keyword idiom — `const { delete: deleteEntry } = { delete: key => {...} }` — object-literal-then-destructure-with-rename for §functions-named-after-reserved-words; the comment `// "delete" is a keyword.` explicitly names §why-the-idiom-is-needed; §borrowable-pattern: §object-literal-then-destructure-with-rename for §functions-named-after-reserved-words; §metrics via §deepCopyJsonable (JSON stringify+parse with optional reviver) + §freezingReviver (applies freeze to each value during reviver pass); §`getMetrics`-returns-a-fresh-frozen-copy-on-every-call — §defensive-clone-on-read; §the-caller-cannot-mutate-the-internal-metrics; §zeroMetrics with §three-named-TODO-future-extensions in comments: (1) method-specific counts (count per has/get/set/delete); (2) liveTouchStats/evictedTouchStats {count, sum, mean, min, max}; (3) p50/p90/p95/p99 via Ben-Haim/Tom-Tov streaming histograms (algorithm citation); §borrowable-pattern: §TODO-comments-with-citations for §future-extensions-that-have-known-implementation-shapes; §sibling-pattern to cycle 197 panic's §three-named-future-extensions and cycle 198 patterns-diagnostic-feedback's §future-helpers-named-not-shipped; §WeakCacheMap-vs-CacheMap Symbol.toStringTag discrimination — `tag = makeMap().clear === undefined ? 'WeakCacheMap' : 'CacheMap'`; §WeakMap-has-no-`clear` (because WeakMaps can't be iterated); §Map-has-`clear`; §so-the-tag-reflects-the-key-strategy; §sibling-pattern to cycle 201 immutable-arraybuffer's §Purposeful-Violation (Symbol.toStringTag for concordance-sniff-defense); §cycle-203-uses-toStringTag-for-informational-discrimination not defense; §cells-not-frozen-because-closely-encapsulated — comment "Instances are not frozen, and so should be closely encapsulated"; §honest-trade-off-named-in-comment with §named-rationale (cells must be mutable for ring operations; so must be closely encapsulated — don't leak them out of the module); §sibling-pattern to cycle 197 panic's §Object.freeze on the export function (freeze-but-not-harden) — both designs §name-the-freeze-discipline-explicitly with §named-rationale-for-the-exception; §capacity-bounded-strict — `if (!isSafeInteger(capacity) || capacity < 0) throw TypeError('capacity must be a non-negative safe integer number <= 2**53 - 1')`; §two-conditions-checked at construction: safe-integer + non-negative; §the-error-message-names-the-implicit-upper-bound greppable for callers who hit it; §sibling-pattern to cycle 199 @endo/nat's §two-different-error-types (TypeError = wrong-kind; RangeError = right-kind-wrong-value) — cycle 203 collapses both into TypeError with combined message; cycle 199 distinguishes them; §seven-freezes: freeze(has), freeze(get), freeze(set), freeze(deleteEntry), freeze(implementation), freeze(kit), freeze(makeCacheMapKit) — each method individually + the implementation + the kit + the factory; §sibling-pattern to cycle 199 memoize's §harden-the-factory-and-the-products at finer granularity; §cycle-203-uses-`freeze`-not-`harden` because it's a §pre-lockdown-utility (sibling to cycle 199 nat's §freeze-as-harden-substitute); §kit-pattern — factory returns `{ cache, getMetrics }` with §named-object-properties; §avoid-array-destructuring (positional) in favor of named for readability; §the-`Kit`-suffix is a convention in @endo/ses-ava and @endo/exo for §factory-returns-multiple-related-things; §borrowable-pattern: §kit-pattern for §factories-returning-multiple-related-objects; §tier-1 borrowing: §bounded-size-cache-with-WeakMap-compatible-interface + §`makeMap`-option-as-key-strategy + §try-as-factory-fall-back-to-constructor + §doubly-linked-ring-with-sentinel-head + §sentinel-head-that-throws-on-direct-access + §touch-moves-to-first-LRU-evicts-last + §LRU-or-better-eviction with named-alternatives + §each-cell-holds-a-SingleEntryMap with §three-strategy-cascade for reset + §UNKNOWN_KEY-sentinel-symbol + §"delete" is a keyword idiom + §deepCopyJsonable + freezingReviver + §metrics-via-defensive-clone-on-read + §TODO-comments-with-citations + §WeakCacheMap-vs-CacheMap toStringTag discrimination + §cells-not-frozen-because-closely-encapsulated + §capacity-bounded-strict + §don't-establish-entry-until-prior-steps-succeed + §seven-freezes + §kit-pattern with named-object-properties + §WeakMap-instances-must-be-replaced-when-key-unknown (information-theoretic limit named); §synthesis-target: slot machine library §bounded-payout-history-cache can §borrow-the-cache-map-kit directly (recent payouts indexed by player-session-token Map-strong-keys or by session-object-reference WeakMap-weak-keys); §doubly-linked-ring-with-sentinel-head borrowable for §ordered-collection-with-LRU-semantics (game-state replay buffer, audit log, recently-used-asset-cache); §LRU-or-better-eviction-with-named-alternatives borrowable as §the-API-contract for §pluggable-cache-policies; §metrics-via-deepCopyJsonable-with-freezingReviver borrowable for any §performance-counters-surface with §defensive-clone-on-read; §kit-pattern borrowable for §factories-returning-multiple-related-objects (cache + metrics; game-state + observer; deck + reshuffle); §TODO-comments-with-citations as §the-shape-for-future-extensions-with-known-implementation-shapes; Endo contributors authored)

### From endo-but-for-bots designs/weblet-next.md (Cycle 204 designs-lane; 454-line Status **Reference** §removed-feature-preservation-document by Kris Kowal (prompted) 2026-03-24; §thirty-eighth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-204; **single most structurally interesting move**: §removed-feature-preservation-document-genre — §a-design-document-as-archaeology distinct from typical design genres (proposal / reference-inventory / reference-pre-emptive-supersedes / in-progress) with §named-purpose "intended as a reference for anyone rebuilding this functionality"; §the-implementation-existed-and-was-deleted; the design captures §what-was for §reconstruction; §Removed-Files-table with 8 files each having §a-named-role: `web-server-node.js` (Unified HTTP/WebSocket server and weblet factory) / `web-server-node-powers.js` (HTTP server powers — port binding, WebSocket upgrade) / `web-page.js` (Browser-side bootstrap — CapTP client, bundle executor) / `interfaces/web.js` (WebPageControllerInterface Exo interface) / `serve-private-port-http.js` (Alternate private-port HTTP server — marked "dead code"; §honest-about-what-was-already-vestigial) / `cli/commands/install.js` (CLI handler for `endo install`) / `cli/commands/open.js` (CLI handler for `endo open`) / `cli/demo/cat.js` (Demo weblet — permission management UI, ~1065 lines); §`@apps`-special-formula was removed from `daemon-node.js` but §the-`specials`-mechanism-itself-is-preserved (defaults to `{}`) — §distinguishing-extension-point-from-extension-content as §the-load-bearing-distinction for §removed-feature-preservation; §Architecture-Overview-with-four-layers each with §named-role: (1) CLI (`endo install`, `endo open`) bundled JS, stored in daemon, evaluated weblet-creation formula, optionally opened URL; (2) Special formula `@apps` (`make-unconfined` formula injected by `daemon-node.js` loading `web-server-node.js` in MAIN worker; available in every host's pet store via `platformNames` spread in `makePetSitter`); (3) Unified server `web-server-node.js` (single HTTP/WebSocket server multiplexes weblets and gateway by hostname dispatch on `Host` header); (4) Browser bootstrap `web-page.js` (loaded in browser, connects back to daemon over WebSocket/CapTP, receives bundle, executes with `importBundle`); §nine-Detailed-Component-Descriptions with uniform template (Entry / Handler / Arguments-Options-table / Flow-numbered-steps / Code-snippets-verbatim) — each component §self-contained-archaeological-fragment; §seven-Patterns-Worth-Preserving as §the-design's-most-valuable-section, each with §a-reusable-shape: (1) §The-`specials`-extension-point — first-class daemon capabilities without per-host configuration; mechanism preserved; `@apps` content removed; (2) §CapTP-over-WebSocket — `makeMessageCapTP` wrapper + `mapWriter(frameWriter, messageToBytes)` + `mapReader(frameReader, bytesToMessage)` provides §a-clean-abstraction-for-CapTP-over-any-byte-stream; `openCapTPSession` helper with §connection-tracking + §heartbeat-ping; (3) §Hostname-based-dispatch — `webletHandlers` map keyed by hostname; §single-HTTP-server-multiplexes-many-applications via §`{respond, connect}`-handler-pairs-per-hostname + §cleanup-on-cancellation; (4) §Access-token-derivation — first 32 chars of formula ID gives §deterministic-unforgeable-token-without-additional-state; used as path prefix for dedicated ports OR hostname key for unified server; §honest-self-critique-in-design-archaeology: "the new design should consider whether hostname-only isolation (via port or virtual host) eliminates the need for explicit tokens"; (5) §Rate-limiting via §per-key-next-allowed-timestamp-with-lazy-sweeping (minimal, zero-dependency; lazy-sweeping cleans stale entries on access rather than via separate timer); (6) §Connection-lifecycle-tracking via §Promise.race-between-transport-close-and-CapTP-close — `trackConnection(Promise.race([connectionClosed, capTpClosed]), ...)`; §connectionClosedPromises-set + §await-all-on-cancellation for §graceful-shutdown; (7) §Browser-endowment-collection — `collectPropsAndBind(window)` traverses prototype chain + binds methods to window + excludes Compartment-conflicting globals; necessary for §any-future-code-providing-browser-like-endowment-set-to-Compartment-based-sandbox; §Note-on-the-Next-Rendition — §forward-looking-shape-without-commitment: §`@webs`-as-directory-of-pet-named-web-applications keyed by pet name; each entry combines §powers + §readable-filesystem producing §a-served-web-application; §readable-tree-as-content-addressed-static-content — §immutable + §content-addressed means §the-server-can-serve-files-without-needing-ongoing-access-to-mutable-storage (just needs the tree's formula identifier); §future-direction: static-and-dynamic-routing-rules + dynamic-content for §applications-defining-server-side-behavior-beyond-static-file-serving; §first-pass-static-serving-from-a-readable-tree as §sufficient-MVP; §Prompt-section-preserves-the-removal-instruction verbatim: "Remove the install command in the CLI and the weblet feature in the daemon. Leave a design, based on insights about the current design, about how to reconstruct this feature. Capture a detailed description of the erstwhile weblet design, such that the portions we have abandoned can be reused" — §three-named-tasks (remove + leave-design + capture-detailed-description); §sibling to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs's §Prompt-section-preserves-discard-prior-design-narrative; §honest-design-evolution-record family extended to §twelve-members with §a-new-shape — §deleted-code's-shape-preserved-for-reconstruction; §at-the-most-extreme-end of honest-design-evolution (other family members preserve unrealized-or-discarded-design-narrative; cycle 204 preserves deleted-code's-shape); §three-different-shapes-of-unrealized-design now in library (cycle 192 engo never shipped; cycle 200 worker-rust-xs discarded mid-design; cycle 204 weblet implemented then removed); §three-different-purposes-for-Reference-status (196 endoclaw inventory-for-comparison; 200 retention-path-notation pre-emptive-supersedes; 204 post-removal-archaeology); §convergence-on-content-addressed-storage: cycle 204's-Note-on-the-Next-Rendition (readable-tree for static content) joins cycle 202's-root-hash-printed-to-stderr (CAS-content-addressing) and cycle 178's-CAS-streaming-snapshot — §three-cycles-converging on §content-addressed-storage as §the-substrate-for-stateless-services in the endor family; §tier-1 borrowing: §removed-feature-preservation-document-genre + §Removed-Files-table-with-named-role-per-file + §Architecture-Overview-with-N-layers + §uniform-Detailed-Component-Descriptions-template + §Patterns-Worth-Preserving-section + §the-`specials`-extension-point + §distinguishing-extension-point-from-extension-content + §CapTP-over-WebSocket + §hostname-based-dispatch + §access-token-derivation-from-formula-ID + §per-key-next-allowed-timestamp-with-lazy-sweeping + §Promise.race-for-first-close-event + §collectPropsAndBind-for-browser-endowment-collection + §Note-on-the-Next-Rendition + §Prompt-section-preserves-maintainer-instruction + §honest-self-critique-in-design-archaeology + §readable-tree-as-content-addressed-static-content + §`@webs`-as-directory-of-pet-named-web-applications; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §removed-feature-archaeology for §previously-experimented-game-modes; §access-token-derivation for §unforgeable-game-session-tokens without per-session state table; §per-key-rate-limiter for §bet-attempt-rate-limiting; §hostname-based-dispatch for §multi-tenant-game-server; §connection-lifecycle-tracking for §game-session-cleanup with multiple close events; §browser-endowment-collection for §game-client-sandbox; §readable-tree-content-addressed for §static-game-assets; Kris Kowal (prompted) authored)
