### From endo packages/evasive-transform/{src/*.js,README.md} (Cycle 205 chat-lane; 697 source across 6 files + ~70 README; §thirty-ninth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-205; §twenty-first-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family; **single most structurally interesting move**: §SES-censorship-evasion via §six-evasion-strategies named-and-exported separately, each targeting §a-specific-Babel-AST-node-type, combined in §a-single-traversal-pass; §the-transform-is-meaning-preserving as §the-load-bearing-discipline; §three-problematic-sequences detected via §a-single-shared-evadeRegexp `/import\s*\(|<!--|-->/g` + §five-context-specific-transforms; §six-evasion-strategies: (1) §evadeStrings — `"import("` → `"im"+"port("` (BinaryExpression concatenation of substrings; the `+ 2` index offset cuts between-first-two-characters of matched sequence); (2) §evadeTemplates — `` `import(` `` → `` `im${''}port(` `` (empty-string-expression as divider in TemplateLiteral.quasis); §TaggedTemplateExpression-excluded because §the-transform-would-not-be-meaning-preserving — the tag function receives cooked/raw arrays of different shapes; §honest-deferred-work-named with PR discussion citation ("It should be possible to wrap the tag with a function that omits expressions we insert, but that's a lot of work to do preemptively"); (3) §evadeRegexpLiteral — `/import(/` → `/i\x69mport/` (hex-escape of first character of each matched sequence); §three-hex-replacements: `i` → `\x69`, `<` → `\x3C`, `-` → `\x2D`; §the-replacement-collection-must-be-maintained-together-with-the-evadeRegexp (explicit comment); (4) §evadeMethod — `EVADE_METHODS = ['import', 'eval']`; Identifier-key → StringLiteral-computed-key for object/class methods named `import` or `eval`; method becomes `['import']()` syntactically; (5) §evadeDecrementGreater — `x-->y` → `(0,x--)>y` — §the-HTML-comment-in-code-edge-case; §`if (a-->b)` is valid JavaScript that means `if ((a--) > b)` but lexically contains `-->` which is §HTML-comment-close-marker SES rejects; §SequenceExpression-wrap inserts `,0` between `x--` and `>`; §the-runtime-semantics-are-preserved (SequenceExpression evaluates left-to-right and returns last value); §the-lexical-sequence-`-->` is broken by `)>`; §a-meaning-preserving-lexical-sequence-breaker; (6) §evadeComment/elideComment — comment-content rewriting (two-mode): §evadeComment defangs with §three-defanging-replacements (`<!--` → `<!=-`, `-->` → `-=>`, `import` → `IMPORT`) + §end-of-comment-marker-defense (`*/` → `*X/`) against block-comment-escape; §coerces-all-comments-to-CommentBlock as §a-normalization-step; §source-comment preserves §the-homoglyph-joke "(featuring homoglyphs for @kriskowal)" — actually it's just uppercase IMPORT not homoglyphs, but the comment reads that way; §source-comments-as-affectionate-jokes for project maintainers; §elideComment strips content (replace non-newlines before last line + replace non-newlines with spaces on last line) with §column-stability so the artifact shrinks dramatically while line/column positions don't shift; §comment-preservation-via-magic-prefix: `!`-prefix (industry-convention `/*!`) + §four-JSDoc-tags `@preserve` + `@copyright` + `@license` + `@cc_on` (Microsoft's "conditional compilation on" for IE — historical curiosity preserved); §adoptStartFrom-with-zero-width-end — `target.loc.end = target.loc.start` so the shorter new node (e.g., "import(<url>)" → "im") doesn't claim too much real estate; §honest-comment names §"trusting in recovery of the actual location immediately afterwards" — the Babel source-map generator recovers the true span via the following AST nodes; §JSON-roundtrip-to-sever-references (`JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(srcLoc))`) for deep-clone of serializable AST location data — sibling-pattern to cycle 203 cache-map's §deepCopyJsonable + freezingReviver (cycle 205 omits the freeze); §try/catch-purely-opportunistic — adoptStartFrom wrapped in try/catch; if anything fails, ignore it; transform proceeds without the location adoption; §failure-mode-is-soft (source-maps might be imperfect but code still works); §sync-and-async-API-pair — `evadeCensor` is trivial async wrapper around `evadeCensorSync` (`async function evadeCensor(...) { return evadeCensorSync(...); }`); §API-symmetry for callers preferring either shape; sibling to cycle 199 trampoline's §sync/async-two-color-sharing-via-generator but simpler shape (operation is genuinely synchronous, not generator-based); §three-overloads-with-JSDoc-narrowing — return type depends on whether `sourceUrl` is provided (TransformedResultWithSourceMap vs TransformedResult); §borrowable-pattern: JSDoc-overloads with narrowing-based-on-option-presence for APIs that return different shapes based on arguments; §Babel-traverse-default-import-workaround — `const traverse = babelTraverse.default || babelTraverse` for §CJS-ESM-interop; some environments (notably `node -r esm`) double-wrap the default export; the fallback chain works in both; §TODO-with-named-future-resolutions: (1) Agoric/agoric-sdk#8671 resolution; (2) upgrade to Babel 8; sibling to cycle 199 @endo/nat's §freeze-as-harden-substitute-pending-PR-#3008 (both packages have §workaround-pending-future-resolution disciplines); §customVisitor escape-hatch — consumers can add their own visitor function to standard transforms (receives same path argument as normal Babel visitor; full Babel API access for §consumer-extensions); §borrowable-pattern: §customVisitor-escape-hatch for §library-extensibility-without-forking; §vestigial-useLocationUnmap-option marked "deprecated, vestigial" in the typedef — §inline-typedef-deprecation-marker as §zero-friction-deprecation-signal; §meaning-preserving-transform as §the-load-bearing-discipline named in the README — §every-transform-must-preserve-runtime-semantics while breaking the §lexical-sequences SES rejects; §source-map-update-discipline — original source map can be passed in to be updated with the transform's mappings; §one-purpose-per-file across six source files (index 132 lines: Public API with three JSDoc overloads each; parse-ast 43: parseAst Babel parser wrapper; transform-ast 82: transformAst orchestrates six strategies via Babel traverse; transform-code 247: evadeStrings + evadeTemplates + evadeRegexpLiteral + evadeMethod + evadeDecrementGreater + adoptStartFrom + addStringToExpressions; transform-comment 81: evadeComment + elideComment + markedForPreservation; generate 112: generate wraps Babel generator with source-map handling) with §named-inter-file-dependencies — §a-layered-module-pattern-not-flat-utility-cluster (cycle 195 cli/src had flat-utility-cluster with no internal deps; cycle 205 has layered with deps; §file-by-purpose discipline shared); §nine-cycles-now-addressing-SES-related-defenses-or-accommodations observation: cycle 175 harden-selector + cycle 183 init+lockdown + cycle 197 panic + cycle 199 memoize + cycle 200 hardened-url-shim + cycle 200 worker-rust-xs + cycle 201 immutable-arraybuffer + cycle 203 cache-map + cycle 205 evasive-transform; §the-@endo-substrate is §richly-SES-aware throughout; §SES-defense-family across §nine-different-axes; §tier-1 borrowing: §SES-censorship-evasion-as-named-design-purpose + §six-evasion-strategies-toolkit + §three-problematic-sequences-single-shared-regex + §comment-defanging-with-three-patterns + §end-of-comment-marker-defense + §HTML-comment-in-code-edge-case-SequenceExpression-wrap + §adoptStartFrom-with-zero-width-end + §JSON-roundtrip-to-sever-references + §try/catch-purely-opportunistic + §sync-and-async-API-pair-with-trivial-async-wrapper + §three-overloads-with-JSDoc-narrowing + §Babel-traverse-default-import-workaround-with-named-future-resolutions + §customVisitor-escape-hatch + §comment-preservation-via-magic-prefix-and-jsdoc-tags + §elideComment-vs-evadeComment-two-mode + §coerces-all-comments-to-CommentBlock + §honest-deferred-work-named-with-PR-discussion-citation + §inline-typedef-deprecation-marker + §one-purpose-per-file-with-named-inter-file-dependencies + §source-map-update-discipline + §meaning-preserving-transform-as-load-bearing-discipline + §homoglyph-joke-as-source-comments-as-affectionate-jokes; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §game-state-source-transform; §SES-censorship-evasion borrowable directly via §evadeCensor pre-evaluation; §customVisitor borrowable for §game-specific-AST-transforms (telemetry hooks, game-specific lints); §elideComment-with-magic-prefix borrowable for §shrinking-game-bundles while §preserving-license-headers; §comment-defanging borrowable for any §source-rewriting that needs §preserve-textual-meaning-while-breaking-lexical-patterns; §HTML-comment-in-code-edge-case borrowable for §JS-source-rewriting needing §preserve-decrement-greater while breaking §lexical-`-->`; §adoptStartFrom-with-zero-width-end borrowable for §AST-rewriting where rewritten-node-is-shorter; §sync-and-async-API-pair borrowable for §APIs-callable-from-both-sync-and-async-contexts; Endo contributors authored)

### From endo-but-for-bots designs/inventory-cancel-and-liveness.md (Cycle 206 designs-lane; 322-line Status **Not Started** design by Kris Kowal (prompted) 2026-02-14 / updated 2026-03-13; §fortieth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-206; **single most structurally interesting move**: §one-button-two-functions (cancel button IS the indicator — single circular 12-14px element whose fill color shows incarnation state, click cancels) + §coalesced-liveness-watcher-protocol with §client-mutates-watched-set + §E.sendOnly-fire-and-forget-watch-unwatch + §confirm-on-cancel-via-two-click-3-second-timeout instead of modal + §deletion-and-cancellation-are-distinct-operations; §five-state-color-coding (Live=Green / Settled=Blue / Pending=Amber-pulsing / Not-incarnated=Gray / Cancelled=Hollow-border) with §one-pulsing-state + §one-non-filled-state as canonical-shape; §The-button-is-always-visible — it is the indicator; §a-tooltip-on-hover shows state in text; §click-cancels via `E(powers).cancel(...petNamePath)`; §the-N+1-subscription-problem named-explicitly: 200 pet names = 200 concurrent remote iterators; §coalesced-watcher-protocol is the §canonical-solution: single async iterator + client-mutated watched-set + server-side filter; §three-method-API on watcher exo + followTransitions + help: §watch(id) + §unwatch(id) + §watchAll(ids) (batch for initial load) + §followTransitions() returns AsyncIterator + §help(); §E.sendOnly-fire-and-forget for watch/unwatch — no round-trip latency on watch-set updates; §watch(id)-immediately-publishes-current-status as §initial-value-on-subscribe pattern; §watcher-scoped-to-agent's-own-formulas as §capability-discipline; §four-lifecycle-hooks for publishing transitions: provide(id)-completes → publish {id, status: 'live'}; context.cancel(reason)-called → publish {id, status: 'cancelled'}; promise-settles → publish {id, status: 'settled'}; watch(id)-called → publish current status (initial); §single-watcher-per-component-tree shared across nested inventories; per-item watch/unwatch from the components; §automatic-GC-on-unmount via CapTP reference drop; §five-step-client-side-lifecycle (1) on mount create watcher (one round-trip), (2) start consuming followTransitions in background loop, (3) on render call E.sendOnly(watcher).watch(id) for each item, (4) on DOM removal call E.sendOnly(watcher).unwatch(id), (5) on unmount drop the watcher's CapTP reference; §confirm-on-cancel via §two-click-3-second-timeout instead of modal — single click enters confirm state (grows slightly, red border, tooltip "Click again to cancel"); second click within 3 seconds executes cancel; click elsewhere or timeout reverts to indicator state; §lightweight-alternative-to-modal-dialogs; §deletion-and-cancellation-are-distinct-operations: × button removes pet name (naming-operation; "I no longer want this name"); indicator button cancels incarnation (lifecycle-operation; "stop this thing"); §cancellation-may-occur-as-deletion-side-effect via GC if formula has no remaining pins/names; §pinned-capabilities-survive-pet-name-deletion via PINS directory — §pin-as-GC-anchor; sibling to cycle 175 harden-selector's §pin-on-first-install discipline (different domain but same §pin-as-anchor concept); §if-other-names-still-reference-the-same-formula, removal deletes only the name and incarnation continues under other names; §disabled-for-special-names (AGENT/SELF/HOST uppercase) — button renders indicator but no click handler; §disabled-but-still-shown for §uncancellable-substrate-capabilities (cancelling AGENT would terminate the agent itself); §ASCII-visual-layout-diagram of inventory rows with three state examples (filled circle / pulsing circle / hollow circle); sibling to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs's §ASCII-architecture-diagram-with-three-process-boxes — both designs use ASCII for §visual-layout-without-rendering-tool-dependency; §IncarnationWatcherI InterfaceGuard with five named methods + help (sibling to cycles 134/136/142/148/150 pass-style-cluster's §interface-guard-validation discipline); §the-watcher-exo-maintains: watchedIds: Set<string> + transitionTopic: topic/publisher; §five-Design-Decisions canonical format: (1) one-button-two-functions; (2) coalesced-watcher-over-per-item-subscriptions; (3) fire-and-forget-watch/unwatch via E.sendOnly; (4) confirm-on-cancel-instead-of-dialog; (5) deletion-and-cancellation-are-distinct; §sibling to cycles 184/188/192/194/196/198/200/202/203/204 §canonical-Design-Decisions-format; §additive-API discipline — new daemon API methods are additive; existing `cancel` CLI command already exercises the underlying mechanism; old clients that don't call the status APIs are unaffected; §sibling to cycle 201 immutable-arraybuffer's §modern-shim-practice-frowns-on-conditional-installation (both designs §preserve-existing-behavior-while-extending); §Upgrade-Considerations-section with two named concerns: crash-recovery-of-status-state + pre-upgrade-worker-status-reporting (workers that were alive before upgrade will need to report status upon first query); §design-consolidation-in-Prompt-section — `live-reference-indicator` prior design folded in: "Consolidated with live-reference-indicator, which proposed status dots with a cancel popover, daemon API options, and security/scaling/upgrade considerations"; §two-designs-merged-into-one as §design-evolution-event; §thirteenth-honest-design-evolution-record family member with §a-new-shape (consolidation rather than removal/revision/discard/Reference-at-landing/etc); §the-algorithm-prefers-shortest-path-discipline with §tie-breaking-with-named-reasons at each tier; §Wikipedia-citation of Shortlex as §formal-name (§citation-of-formal-name as §design-archaeology); §four-stages of comparison (undefined-handling + array-length + cumulative-length + lexicographic + return-0); §each-stage-returns-early on difference; §each-stage-has-a-named-reason-comment in source: "Undefined compares greater than anything else" / "Prefer the shortest dependency path" / "Otherwise, favor the shortest cumulative length" / "Otherwise, compare lexicographically"; §undefined-compares-greater-than-anything-else matches `Array.prototype.sort` default behavior; §three-mermaid-graphs in README (actually four numbered examples) each demonstrating §one-tier-of-the-comparison driving the choice: (1) trivial linear `entry → foo → bar → baz`; (2) length-tier example (length 3 vs length 4); (3) cumulative-length-tier example (`foobarbaz` vs `fooalternativebaz`); (4) lex-tier example (`['foo','spam','baz']` vs `['foo','quux','baz']` where `quux` < `spam` at first code unit of second element); §honest-Note-about-lexicographic-surprises: "the 'lexicographic' comparison uses the UTF-16 code unit order, and thus may be surprising"; §honest-disclosure that lexicographic comparison is not Unicode collation but UTF-16 code unit order; §named-deviation-from-natural-expectation; §sanity-check-with-c8-ignore-comment (`/* c8 ignore next 5 */`) — §defensive-check-that-should-never-fire (previous tier would have returned); §c8-coverage-tool-ignore-comment tells coverage tool not to count the five lines as uncovered; §the-check-is-real-protection but the coverage tool shouldn't penalize it; §CompareFn typedef with §JSDoc-callback shape: `@template T` + `@callback CompareFn` + `@param @returns` — §generic-typedef-CompareFn<T> reused for §both-exports with different T (`CompareFn<string>` for stringCompare; `CompareFn<string[]|undefined>` for pathCompare); §JSDoc-callback-syntax creates §a-named-reusable-function-type; §stringCompare as building block — `const stringCompare = (a, b) => (a === b ? 0 : a < b ? -1 : 1)` using JavaScript's `<` operator (UTF-16 code unit order); §building-block-and-derived-form; §the-no-nested-ternary-eslint-disable named-in-source; §eslint-aware-named-deviation; §used-by-compartment-mapper for shortest path to transitive dependency — §canonical-consumer named-explicitly; §algorithm-isn't-abstract; §it's-the-tie-breaker for dependency-resolution; §sibling-pattern to cycle 199 memoize's §passStyleOf-cited-as-canonical-memoize-user; §q = JSON.stringify canonical shorthand (sibling to cycle 207 env-options); §the-`pathCompare`-package is §the-substrate-for-cycle-200's-best-path-selection-rule via @endo/compartment-mapper's use case (though cycle 200 design has its own four-tier-comparison adapted to retention-path semantics); ### From endo-but-for-bots designs/lal-fae-form-provisioning.md (Cycle 210 designs-lane; 713-line Status **Complete** design by Kris Kowal (prompted) 2026-03-02 / updated 2026-03-05; all four phases shipped; §forty-fourth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-210; **single most structurally interesting move**: §three-layer-lifecycle (Setup-script / Manager-agent / Worker-loops) with §ASCII-diagram-of-fan-out-pattern + §Architectural-Constraint-named-in-section-header (§"Guest Cannot Create Guests") + §three-option-analysis (Eval-Proposal / Manager-Asks-@host / Grant-@agent-Host-Power) §resolved-to-Option-C with §named-rationale + §the-form-submission-from-@host-already-serves-as-the-consent-mechanism + §inbox-as-durable-config-store; §the-design-encodes-a-consent-boundary: §the-form-submission-from-@host serves as §the-explicit-user-consent for each new guest creation; §the-@agent-introduction-bypasses-eval-proposal-friction while §preserving-the-consent-property; §three-named-problems with §explicit-user-facing-pain: (1) Configuration baked in at provisioning (changing model or API key requires re-running setup); (2) Only one agent identity per install (each setup.js creates exactly one guest); (3) No user consent flow (agent starts following inbox immediately); §three-layer-lifecycle each with named role: (1) Setup-script provisions a single "manager" guest profile with no LLM configuration; (2) Manager-agent sends configuration form to @host on startup, on each value reply creates new guest profile and spawns agentic loop; (3) Worker-loops follow specific guest's inbox, processes messages using LLM configuration from form submission; §ASCII-diagram shows fan-out from @host → Manager → multiple Guest workers; §sibling-pattern to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs's §ASCII-architecture-diagram-with-three-process-boxes; §Architectural-Constraint-named-in-section-header — provideGuest method is on EndoHost interface, not EndoGuest; §a-guest-caplet-cannot-directly-create-new-guest-profiles; §the-constraint-named-explicitly so the design must resolve it; §three-option-analysis for resolution: Option A Evaluate-Proposal (manager sends evaluate proposal to @host; @host reviews and grants; drawback: user must grant every guest creation via eval-proposal — adds friction); Option B Manager-Asks-@host (manager sends form, @host submits, manager doesn't create guest itself, sends request to @host for guest with that name; drawback: requires new capability or multi-step request/response flow); Option C Grant-the-Manager-a-Host-Power (CHOSEN — setup script introduces `@agent` power via introducedNames so manager can call E(agent).provideGuest); §two-named-reasons for Option C: (1) already-the-pattern-used-in-setup.js-today; (2) the-form-submission-from-@host-already-serves-as-the-consent-mechanism — @host chooses to submit the form, which triggers guest creation; §the-consent-property-is-preserved without §the-eval-proposal-friction; §sibling-pattern to cycle 208 familiar-bundled-agents's §three-option-analysis for §The-Powers-Problem — both cycle 208 and cycle 210 are §sibling-designs with §parallel-three-option-analyses on §related-axes; §inbox-as-durable-config-store: §"No explicit config persistence needed. followMessages() replays all historical messages on restart, including past value submissions. The manager re-processes each submission: provideGuest is idempotent (returns the existing guest), and the worker loop is respawned. The inbox itself serves as the durable config store"; §three-pieces-fit-together: (1) followMessages-replays-all-historical-messages-on-restart; (2) provideGuest-is-idempotent (calling provideGuest('ada', ...) twice returns same guest both times); (3) manager-re-processes-each-submission (replay creates same guests, spawns same worker loops); §the-result: §no-explicit-config-persistence-needed; §the-inbox-IS-the-config-database; §the-daemon's-existing-message-durability suffices; §sibling-pattern to cycle 202 endor-run-expanded's §root-hash-printed-to-stderr-for-re-run and cycle 175 harden-selector's §pin-on-first-install (§use-existing-mechanism-as-anchor); §per-worker-provider: each worker creates own LLM provider from form submission's host/model/authToken; §different-workers-can-use-different-models-or-providers simultaneously; §manager-worker-split discipline with §three-named-benefits: (1) Manager's-inbox-is-clean (only form submissions); (2) Each-worker-has-its-own-identity; (3) Each-worker-has-its-own-inbox-and-pet-store; §single-responsibility-per-caplet (manager handles provisioning; worker handles conversation); §shared-form-fields between Lal and Fae (name + host + model + authToken); difference is worker loop behavior (Lal: reply-chain-transcripts + static tools; Fae: flat-transcripts + dynamic-tool-discovery); §four-form-fields table; §three-Alternatives-Considered each rejected with named reason: (1) Environment-Variables-with-Optional-Form-Override REJECTED (preserves "one-agent-per-install" limitation; doesn't exercise form system as primary configuration path); (2) Eval-Proposal-for-Guest-Creation REJECTED (more restrictive; adds friction; rejected in favor of simpler @agent introduction); (3) Unconfined-Worker-Caplets DEFERRED (better isolation but more complex; §fast-path-then-slow-path-for-diagnostic-quality (fast length-check; slow find-and-throw on collision); (4) ident-checker.js — DEPRECATED with §forwarding-comment to Rejector confirm/reject pattern; §two-deprecation-tags on one file (Checker typedef + identChecker); §sibling to cycle 205 evasive-transform's §inline-typedef-deprecation-marker; (5) list-difference.js — Set-based difference using §SameValueZero-comparison-noted-explicitly (NaN equality, ±0 equality); §used-for-name-mismatch-diagnostics; (6) make-iterator.js — §hardening-analog of Array.prototype[Symbol.iterator]; §self-iterable via `[Symbol.iterator]: () => iter`; §one-shot-iterator; (7) make-array-iterator.js — concrete factory built on makeIterator; §terminal-value-doesn't-matter @ts-expect-error with §rationale-comment ("The terminal value doesn't matter"); §borrowable-pattern: §@ts-expect-error-with-rationale-comment for §named-acceptable-type-errors; (8) object-map.js — objectMap + objectExtendEach value-level transformations + typedEntries/typedMap/fromTypedEntries JSDoc-typed-casts for built-ins; §five-named-edge-cases per utility in doc-comments as §runtime-spec; §the-typedMap is §uncurry-this on Array.prototype.map — §three-canonical-uncurry-shapes in @endo now observed: cycle 199 bind.bind(bind.call) + cycle 207 Reflect.apply-form + cycle 211 Function.prototype.call.bind; §sibling-pattern to cycle 209 path-compare's §JSDoc-callback-typedef-with-generic-T (both packages use JSDoc-typedef as typed-cast for generic-type-narrowing); §hideAndHardenFunction on every export with semantic distinction: §hideAndHardenFunction-for-wrappers (error-relabeling functions because they're not the source of the error and shouldn't appear in stack traces) + §harden-for-leaf-utilities (pure utilities because they are the call); cycle 211 common (low-level shared layer); §the-three-cluster-shapes complement each other; §tier-1 borrowing: §ten-utility-files-with-one-purpose-per-file + §tree-shaking-friendly-architecture + §four-named-inclusion-criteria for utility packages + §applyLabelingError-as-substrate-for-rich-diagnostic-tooling + §throwLabeled-companion-with-X-Caused-by-template + §sync-and-async-error-relabeling-in-one-function + §fast-path-then-slow-path-for-diagnostic-quality + §fromUniqueEntries-defends-against-user-data-property-name-injection + §deprecation-tags-with-forwarding-comment-to-replacement-pattern + §SameValueZero-comparison-noted-explicitly + §hardening-analog-of-built-in-iterators + §self-iterable-via-Symbol.iterator-returns-self + §@ts-expect-error-with-rationale-comment + §four-shape-toolkit-value-vs-descriptor-times-map-vs-extend + §five-named-edge-cases-per-utility + §JSDoc-typed-cast for built-ins with loose default types + §three-canonical-uncurry-shapes in @endo + §hideAndHardenFunction-for-wrappers-vs-harden-for-leaf-utilities + §honest-cross-package-TypeScript-edges-with-explanatory-comments + §generic-named-package-with-named-membership-rules; ### From endo-but-for-bots designs/outliner-design-doc.md (Cycle 212 designs-lane; 182-line design with no Status/Author frontmatter; appears Proposed-or-Reference; §forty-sixth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-212; **single most structurally interesting move**: §Type-3-chat-system (Type 1 chat / Type 2 forum / Type 3 outliner) + §immutability-at-protocol-level with §every-action-as-typed-reply + §seven-built-in-reply-types + §user-defined-reply-types-as-string-tags-no-protocol-changes-needed + §last-write-wins-with-edit-queue-as-resolution-mechanism + §private-reply-trees-as-capability-grant-with-layered-confidentiality + §viewer-side-administration-with-retroactive-blocking + §Roam-on-Endo-Petdaemon-replaces-Automerge-CRDTs-with-object-capability-message-passing; §the-design's-load-bearing-axis: §every-mutation-is-immutable-and-attributed; §what-the-user-experiences-as-an-edit is §a-typed-reply that the renderer interprets; §seven-built-in-reply-types (Reply child-node / Edit replacement-text / Deletion disregard-target / Move reorder-among-siblings / Pro Kialo-style-argument-in-favor / Con argument-against / Supporting-Evidence references); §user-defined-reply-types: "channel administrator can add new reply type strings at runtime [...] reply type is just a string tag on a reply message — no protocol changes needed"; §extension-via-string-tag-without-protocol-change as §the-design-axiom; §last-write-wins-with-edit-queue-as-resolution-mechanism: no locking, no OT, no CRDT (explicit rejection of CRDT/OT approach); §the-edit-queue-IS-the-resolution-mechanism; §earlier-edits-not-lost; §sibling-pattern to cycle 200 retention-path-notation's §best-path-selection-rule and cycle 209 path-compare's §three-tier-comparison; §private-reply-trees: any reply (of any type) restricted to subset of participants at creation time; §the-restricted-reply-and-its-entire-subtree-are-visible-only-to-named-participants; §maps-directly-to-Endo's-capability-model; §author-side-access-control distinct from §viewer-side-blocking; §layered-confidentiality: private subtrees within private subtrees with narrower participant sets; §sibling-pattern to cycle 161 daemon-capability-filesystem's §Bazel-style-selective-dependency-mounting (§absence-is-structural-not-policy); §edit-queue with deletion-of-deletions: recursive deletion targeting; deletions appear in queue and can themselves be deleted, restoring the edit they targeted; §the-renderer-walks-the-queue and §skips-deleted-edits-and-deleted-deletions; §avatar-lineage as §partially-overlapping-circular-avatars-ordered-left-to-right-by-edit-sequence; §z-index-as-edit-order encoding (original poster leftmost + lowest z-index; most recent editor rightmost + highest z-index); §clicking-the-avatar-array-opens-the-edit-queue (visual is also the affordance); §Global-Playback chronological walk-through of every reply-type message since last visit; §playback-with-asymmetric-display: seen-before nodes get diff-markup (additions highlighted, deletions struck through); first-time nodes get current-content-only with avatar-lineage and "Edited by" attribution; §two-named-affordances: automatically-on-visit-after-absence + on-demand-at-any-time; §viewer-side-administration with §retroactive-blocking: blocking excludes user's edits from viewer's rendered state; §applied-retroactively (slices out mutations from viewer's entire history); §block-propagates-to-future-invitees; §creates-personalized-unique-view per viewer ("each viewer's document state reflects their own trust decisions"); §there-is-no-canonical-view — only viewer-specific-views; §sibling-pattern to cycle 196 endoclaw's §object-capability-vs-ambient-authority — both designs reject centralized authority for per-viewer or per-capability decisions; §custom-attenuation-code in SES-Compartment: "Even untrusted attenuator code can at worst interrupt invitees' participation — it cannot escalate privileges"; §the-confinement-property; §sibling-pattern to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs + cycle 205 evasive-transform — three designs relying on SES-Compartment substrate; §reference-scoping no-upward-traversal: "Having a reference to a message does not grant reference to its parent. This follows the file-system principle: endowing a directory does not endow upward"; "You can give the bag and know that nothing but what's in the bag is getting handed over"; §the-canonical-OCap-metaphor; §sibling-pattern to cycle 161 daemon-capability-filesystem and cycle 166 daemon-mount — three designs sharing the no-upward-traversal discipline at three layers; §agent-participation as first-class: "any Agent holding a channel capability can participate"; agents and humans use the same interface; §@mention-as-wake-up-command — "the mention acts as a wake-up command. The Agent receives its current view of the document and decides how to act"; §explicit-mentions-important in multi-user real-time collaboration (boundary between brainstorm and trigger); §Roam-on-Endo-Petdaemon: "The Outliner is effectively Roam implemented on Endo Petdaemon. It replaces Automerge CRDTs with Endo's object-capability message-passing model"; §what-it-gains-vs-loses-with-pragmatic-substitute explicit trade-off: gains (fine-grained permissions, edit provenance, viewer-side administration, private reply trees, programmable attenuation, global playback, Endo invitation system); loses (real-time collaborative text editing within a single block — Automerge/NextGraph's CRDT strength); pragmatic substitute (last-write-wins + review via edit queue); §Meta+J keyboard shortcut for new top-level node/channel (named-keyboard-shortcut in design doc); §eight-Open-Questions: avatar-lineage depth / agent thinking-loading states reply type / concurrent Move conflict resolution / user-defined reply type governance / Pro/Con special rendering / max nesting depth for deletions-of-deletions / private-tree-visibility expansion after creation / Global-Playback automatic-vs-on-demand threshold; §design-maturity-signal: eight-Open-Questions is between cycle 198's one-Open-Question (nearly-ready) and cycle 196's seven-Open-Questions (reference); cycle 212 has eight, suggesting mid-stage design; §tier-1 borrowing: §Type-3-chat-system as named-third-instance in a chat-system family + §immutability-at-protocol-level + §typed-replies-as-rendered-mutations + §extensible-typed-replies-via-string-tags (renderer-as-interpreter) + §last-write-wins-with-edit-queue-as-resolution-mechanism + §private-reply-trees-as-capability-grant + §layered-confidentiality + §recursive-mutation-of-typed-replies + §queue-walk-by-renderer + §z-index-as-temporal-order-encoding + §playback-with-viewer-history-aware-display + §viewer-side-administration-with-retroactive-blocking + §block-propagation-creates-personalized-unique-view + §custom-attenuation-code-in-SES-Compartment + §reference-scoping-no-upward-traversal (file-system principle "you can give the bag and know that nothing but what's in the bag is getting handed over") + §unified-Agent-interface-for-humans-and-bots + §@mention-as-wake-up-command + §what-it-gains-vs-loses-with-pragmatic-substitute + §named-keyboard-shortcut in design doc + §eight-Open-Questions as design-maturity-signal; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §collaborative-game-design-document — §immutability-at-protocol-level + §typed-replies for game-modification-history (every game-rule edit is a typed-reply with attribution); §last-write-wins + edit-queue for concurrent-rule-changes-by-multiple-designers; §private-reply-trees for unreleased-game-variants-shared-with-subset-of-players; §viewer-side-administration-with-retroactive-blocking for player-trust-decisions; §reference-scoping for game-component-references that don't grant access to parent game; §@mention-as-wake-up-command for triggering-game-bots in design discussions; §what-it-gains-vs-loses-with-pragmatic-substitute for game-design-trade-offs against existing game engines; ### From endo packages/stream-node/{reader.js,writer.js,index.js,README.md} (Cycle 213 chat-lane; 150 source + 1 README; §forty-seventh consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-213; §twenty-fifth-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family; **single most structurally interesting move**: §two-files-for-two-directions (reader.js + writer.js) + §Buffer-to-Uint8Array zero-copy conversion via mapReader from @endo/stream + §readableObjectMode-and-readableEncoding-guards with named-error-messages + §Stream-must-have-return-and-throw (stricter than AsyncIterator) + §Promise.race-with-finalIteration in writer.next for concurrent-completion-sources + §three-Node-event-handlers (error / finish / close) with §sink-for-Node-14-unhandled-error-race-defense + §back-pressure-via-await-on-write; §two-named-compensations: Buffer-becomes-Uint8Array + iterable-becomes-hardened-Stream; §the-load-bearing-discipline: adapt Node.js streams (Buffer values, not hardened) to Endo's async-iterable streams (Uint8Array, hardened); §the-comment-names-the-rationale: "This module provided for sake of fewer head scratches. Node.js readable streams satisfy the signature of an async iterable iterator. They however iterate Node.js Buffer values and are not hardened, so this implementation compensates for both"; §readableObjectMode-and-readableEncoding guards at start of makeNodeReader with named-error-messages: "Cannot convert Node.js object mode Reader to AsyncIterator<Uint8Array>" / "Cannot convert Node.js Reader with readableEncoding ${q(...)} to a AsyncIterator<Uint8Array>"; §Stream-must-have-return-and-throw stricter than AsyncIterator — JavaScript AsyncIterator allows return/throw to be optional; Endo Stream requires them; §the-adapter-fills-in-the-gaps; §iterator.return preserved via `assert(iterator.return)` before use; §input.destroy(error) on throw propagates Stream error to underlying Node Reader; §self-referential-asyncIterator via `[Symbol.asyncIterator]() { return reader; }` (sibling to cycle 211 common's makeIterator); §Buffer-to-Uint8Array zero-copy: `new Uint8Array(buffer.buffer, buffer.byteOffset, buffer.length)` — shares underlying ArrayBuffer; §defense-in-depth-assert-typeof-buffer-is-not-string (already guarded above); §makeNodeWriter — Promise.race-with-finalIteration with three completion paths: (1) finalIteration completes (error/finish/close); (2) writer.write returns false → wait-for-drain → resolve as non-final-iteration; (3) writer.write returns true → resolve immediately as non-final-iteration; §the-callback-can-also-reject if writer.write callback reports error; §three-Node-event-handlers wired in finalIteration Promise constructor: error → reject; finish → resolve as done; close → resolve as done; §cleanup-after-first-fire: writer.off all three + writer.on('error', sink) to defang post-cleanup errors; §honest-comment-on-redundant-watch: "Streams should emit either error or finish and then may emit close. So, watching close is redundant but makes us feel safer"; §sink-for-Node-14-unhandled-error-race-defense: `const sink = harden(() => {})` listener after cleanup; §named-Node-version-compat-hack with explanatory comment "Prevent Node 14 from triggering a global unhandled error if we race"; §sibling-pattern to cycle 199 nat's §Apps-Script-bigint-literal-workaround (named-runtime-compatibility-hack with explanatory comment); §back-pressure-via-await-on-write: "Back pressure emerges from awaiting on the promise returned by `next` before calling `next` again"; §the-caller-must-await-each-next before calling next again; §if-the-Node-stream-is-applying-back-pressure (write returns false), §the-promise-doesn't-resolve-until-drain; §writer.write-callback-and-drain-coordination — explicit back-pressure mechanism; §pre-hardened-nonFinalIterationResult-constant: `const nonFinalIterationResult = harden({ done: false, value: undefined })` — single pre-hardened object returned by every successful write; §no-allocation-per-call; §sibling-pattern to cycle 197 panic's §lastResortError (pre-frozen exported constant for identity-equality checks); §Fail-on-write-after-finalized: `!finalized || Fail\`Cannot write into closed Node stream\`` — pre-condition on every next call; §hybrid-async-iterator-plus-generator-as-named-Writer-shape (in writer.js comment: "Adapts a Node.js stream to an Writer<Uint8Array>, where a writer stream is modeled as a hybrid async iterator + generator"); §three-different-runtime-version-or-environment-compat-hacks now in library: cycle 199 nat (Apps-Script-bigint-literal-workaround BigInt(0) not 0n) + cycle 205 evasive-transform (Babel-traverse-default-import-workaround `babelTraverse.default || babelTraverse`) + cycle 213 stream-node (Node-14-unhandled-error-race-defense `writer.on('error', sink)` after cleanup); §two-different-uses-of-Promise.race for multiple-completion-sources: cycle 204 weblet-next (transport-close-vs-CapTP-close) + cycle 213 stream-node (finalIteration in writer.next); §tier-1 borrowing: §adapter-between-incompatible-shapes-with-pre-condition-checks-with-named-error-messages + §Buffer-to-Uint8Array-zero-copy-conversion + §Stream-must-have-return-and-throw-stricter-than-AsyncIterator + §self-referential-asyncIterator + §iterator.return-preserved-via-assert + §input.destroy(error)-on-throw + §Promise.race-with-finalIteration + §three-Node-event-handlers-with-honest-comment-about-defensive-redundancy + §cleanup-after-first-fire + §sink-for-Node-14-unhandled-error-race-defense + §back-pressure-via-await-on-write + §writer.write-callback-and-drain-coordination + §pre-hardened-constants-for-frequently-returned-values + §Fail-on-write-after-finalized + §hybrid-async-iterator-plus-generator-as-named-Writer-shape; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §game-event-stream-adapter — Buffer-to-Uint8Array borrowable for any adapter-between-Node-and-Endo-streams; Promise.race-with-finalIteration borrowable for game-event-streams with multiple completion sources (player disconnect / game over / timeout); back-pressure-via-await borrowable for rate-limiting-events-naturally via iterator-protocol contract; three-event-handlers with cleanup-after-first-fire for robust-stream-finalization; pre-hardened-constants for hot-path-iteration-results; ### From endo-but-for-bots designs/lal-reply-chain-transcripts.md (Cycle 214 designs-lane; 531-line Status **Complete** design by Kris Kowal (prompted) 2026-02-26 / updated 2026-03-05; Phases 1-4 shipped + Phase 5 extracted to separate design lal-transcript-memory-management; §forty-eighth consecutive designs/chat alternation cycle 166-214; **single most structurally interesting move**: §linked-chain-of-transcript-nodes with §shared-prefix-stored-once + §branching-is-free + §two-phase-node-lifecycle (Phase 1 inbound + Phase 2 own-outbound-alias) + §honest-design-evolution-visible-in-document (the §"This is getting complex. Let's simplify:" §self-correcting-prose); §the-data-model is §the-load-bearing-design-move: rather than storing a full copy of the message array for each branch, transcripts are represented as a singly-linked chain of nodes; §each-node-stores-only-the-messages-appended-at-that-step + §a-pointer-to-the-parent-node; §the-shared-prefix-is-stored-once; §five-numbered-goals in Motivation (each-reply-chain-independent-LLM-transcript / stand-alone-message-creates-new-transcript / multiple-replies-to-same-parent-share-common-prefix-without-duplicating / agent-uses-reply-instead-of-send / outgoing-messages-carry-transcript-depth-indicator); §TranscriptNode typedef: messageId + parentMessageId + messages (LLM messages appended at this step only) + optional lastInboxNumber; §branching-is-free: when two replies target the same parent messageId, each creates a new node pointing to the same parent; §the-shared-prefix-stored-once; §ASCII-tree-diagram of root → node M1 → branches M3 + M4 (sibling to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs / cycle 206 inventory-cancel-and-liveness / cycle 210 lal-fae-form-provisioning §ASCII-art-as-design-prose); §assembly-via-walk-from-leaf-to-root: chain = []; current = leafMessageId; while current !== null: node = store.get(current); chain.push(node.messages); current = node.parentMessageId; chain.reverse(); return chain.flat(); §the-self-correcting-prose walks through four iterations: (1) naive shape (create node on inbound, then create index-node on outbound); (2) honest-observation "Wait — this creates an asymmetry"; (3) cleaner alternative (each agentic loop iteration produces a node containing user + assistant + tools); (4) honest-observation "This is getting complex. Let's simplify:"; (5) §Revised: §Two-Phase Node Lifecycle (the final design); §Phase-1-on-inbound: create node keyed by inbound messageId, chained to parent, append user message, run agentic loop appending assistant + tool messages; §Phase-2-on-own-outbound: create alias mapping outboundId → same node as replyTo (so future replies via outbound messageId find the chain); §fifteenth-honest-design-evolution-record family member with §a-new-shape: §design-evolution-visible-in-the-prose (the iteration is part of the document, not just a Prompt-section narrative or a Status-section pivot); §sibling to cycle 198 patterns-diagnostic-feedback's §three-revision-pivots-visible-in-Prompt-section (cycle 198 records pivots after the fact; cycle 214 records iteration in real-time-in-the-prose); §durable-storage in pet store: `transcript-<messageId>` pet name; in-memory Map as cache; pet store as source of truth; §lazy-load-on-cache-miss in getNode (check cache → check pet store has → lookup → store in cache → return); §avoids-unbounded-heap-growth (only actively-referenced nodes remain in cache; idle conversation chains evicted to pet store; simple LRU or size-bounded cache can limit in-memory set — deferred to Phase 5); §sibling to cycle 203 cache-map and cycle 199 memoize (three different cache shapes at three different layers); §depth-as-text-prefix `[depth:N] <actual message text>`; §depth counts user + assistant turns excluding system prompt and tool-call results; §no-daemon-schema-changes-required; §text-prefix-as-out-of-band-metadata when schema changes are expensive; §UI-can-parse-and-extract-it ("turn 12") or render-verbatim-if-not-parsed; §sibling to cycle 202 endor-run-expanded's §root-hash-printed-to-stderr (both use existing text channels for out-of-band data); §Algorithm section gives pseudo-code for Message Arrival + Agentic Loop per-node + Handling Own Messages; §honest-comment naming the asymmetry then revising to two-phase lifecycle; §No-daemon-changes-required — "The daemon already supports reply(), storeValue(), messageId/replyTo on messages, and followMessages() including own messages. All needed infrastructure exists"; §three-Alternatives-Considered each rejected: (1) Full-Transcript-Copy-Per-Branch — wasteful (long conversations with many branches duplicate the entire prefix); (2) Single-Transcript-with-Context-Tags — fragile (LLM may confuse contexts, wastes context window); (3) Depth-as-Structured-Message-Field — requires daemon schema changes/migration/validation; deferred; §four-Phases-all-Complete: Phase 1 Reply Tool and Threading / Phase 2 Node-Based Transcript Store / Phase 3 Durable Storage / Phase 4 Depth Indicator; §Phase-5-Memory-Management extracted to separate design (lal-transcript-memory-management.md); §borrowable-pattern: §Phase-N-extracted-to-separate-design when phase is substantive enough; §sibling to cycle 210 lal-fae-form-provisioning's §four-Phases-all-Complete; §Decisions-Made-vs-Tentative-Decisions two-table-shape — Decisions Made (firm): transcript-structure / node-identity / branching / durable-storage / reply-tool / own-message-handling / depth-communication / outbound-messageId; Tentative Decisions (may adjust during implementation): in-memory-cache (unbounded initially, add LRU in Phase 5) / transcript-assembly (walk-and-flat on every LLM call) / node-granularity (one per inbound + agentic loop output) / alias-storage (duplicate under both messageIds); §borrowable-pattern: two-table-shape for §designs-where-some-decisions-are-still-pending-implementation-verification; §sibling to cycle 188 daemon-rust-xs-performance's §Working-copy-inventory section and cycle 198's §nine-Design-Decisions; §four-out-of-scope items: transcript-summarization-or-sliding-window (future memory management) / chat-UI-rendering-of-depth-badges ([depth:N] is human-readable) / reply-chain-visualization (covered by chat-reply-chain-visualization.md) / concurrent-agentic-loops (agent processes one message at a time); §three-completed-Lal/Fae-cluster-designs now in library: cycle 208 familiar-bundled-agents (delivery) + cycle 210 lal-fae-form-provisioning (configuration) + cycle 214 lal-reply-chain-transcripts (transcript memory); §a-three-design-cluster for the Lal feature; §five-different-uses-of-Alternatives-Considered-or-equivalent now observed (cycle 198 interleaved + cycle 200 retention-path collected + cycle 208 three-option-analysis + cycle 210 both-shapes + cycle 214 three-rejected); §rich-family-of-rhetorical-shapes for §recording-rejected-alternatives; §tier-1 borrowing: §five-numbered-goals-named-in-Motivation + §linked-chain-of-transcript-nodes-with-shared-prefix-stored-once + §branching-is-free + §ASCII-tree-diagram of branching + §assembly-via-walk-from-leaf-to-root + §two-Phase-Node-Lifecycle with alias-mapping + §honest-design-evolution-visible-in-the-prose (self-correcting prose as new rhetorical shape) + §pet-store-as-source-of-truth-with-in-memory-cache + §lazy-load-on-cache-miss + §avoids-unbounded-heap-growth + §depth-as-text-prefix + §text-prefix-as-out-of-band-metadata + §no-daemon-changes-required + §three-Alternatives-Considered each rejected + §four-Phases-all-Complete + §Phase-N-extracted-to-separate-design + §Decisions-Made-vs-Tentative-Decisions two-table-shape + §four-out-of-scope-items + §record-of-what-was-built; ### From agoric-labs/agoric-to-axelar-local agoric-docs/ymax-evm-remote-accounts.md (Cycle 214 user-requested side-ingest 2026-06-07; ~270 lines with five mermaid diagrams; §first-Agoric-Labs-design-ingested into the library; §independently-audited by Atredis Partners 2026 with zero critical/high/medium/low findings and three informational findings (one fixed in router implementation); **single most structurally interesting move**: §assets-should-outlive-execution-paths (separate-asset-holding-contracts-from-execution-machinery; asset side is boring and durable; execution side is replaceable) + §EIP-1167-minimal-proxy-Remote-Accounts with §deterministic-address-derivation-from-three-inputs (Remote-Account-factory-address + immutable-Remote-Account-implementation-address + Ymax-portfolio-Agoric-account-address) + §race-tolerant-idempotent-provisioning via §the-deterministic-address-IS-the-coordination-primitive + §three-state-router-authorization-state-machine (Unknown → Vetted → Authorized) with §two-factor-mechanism (EVM-multisig-vets + Ymax-orchestration-via-existing-authorized-router-authorizes) + §self-dispatch-pattern with §msg.sender==address(this)-check + §calldata-rewrite-replacing-txId-with-authenticated-source-address (same-length-encoding) + §resolvers-as-bounded-fact-reporters (one bit per instruction; not arbitrary state) + §finality-checks-before-settlement; §three-named-properties preserved in user intent flow: (1) user's-assets-segregated-from-other-users / (2) system-does-not-take-custody-via-shared-omnibus / (3) user-does-not-need-to-manually-operate-accounts-on-every-destination-chain; §the-key-architectural-choice quoted: "separate the contracts that hold assets from the machinery used to operate them"; §asset-holding-side needs to be boring-and-durable (stable address over time; auditable in block explorers); §execution-side has different-pressures (cross-chain messaging providers change; instruction formats evolve; new ways of interacting with EVM accounts become useful); §those-changes-should-be-applicable-to-existing-portfolio-accounts (not requiring every portfolio to move assets into a new account); §Remote-Account-as-stable-object: everything-else is organized around safely-creating-those-accounts + sending-authenticated-instructions-to-them + observing-the-results; §EIP-1167 minimal-proxy: each account reuses shared implementation while having its own address; §deterministic-address-derivation supports race-tolerant-idempotent-provisioning ("the same portfolio for the same Ymax instance on the same EVM chain always maps to the same Remote Account address; the system can safely check whether the account already exists, deploy it using the Remote Account factory if needed, and independently start moving assets to computed deposit destinations"); §borrowable-pattern: §the-deterministic-address-IS-the-coordination-primitive; §sibling to cycle 210 lal-fae-form-provisioning's §inbox-as-durable-config-store + provideGuest-is-idempotent — both designs use idempotent-provisioning-with-a-deterministic-name; §routers as authenticated-instruction-delivery (not dumb pipes): "the router is not just a dumb pipe to Remote Accounts. It exposes high-level instructions that the Ymax orchestration contract uses to provision accounts, process deposits, execute account operations, and administer router authorization"; §messaging-layer-or-router-itself-must-ensure-messages-are-authentic-non-replayable-tied-to-correct-source-account; §confused-deputy-defense; §sibling to cycle 196 endoclaw's §object-capability-vs-ambient-authority and cycle 212 outliner-design-doc's §reference-scoping-no-upward-traversal (all three designs §confine-authority-at-each-layer); §three-state-router-authorization-state-machine with named transitions: Unknown → vetRouter (EVM multisig) → Vetted; Vetted → authorizeRouter (Ymax via other authorized router) → Authorized; Authorized → deauthorizeRouter (Ymax via other authorized router) → Vetted; Vetted → unvetRouter (EVM multisig) → Unknown; §two-factor-mechanism (different-authority-per-transition); §critical-security-property: "compromise of a single router, or even the compromise of an underlying cross-chain messaging system, should not by itself authorize a completely new control path for all Remote Accounts"; §honest-disclosure-of-residual-trust-boundaries: "An already authorized router remains highly privileged, so router code and messaging authentication are still critical trust boundaries"; §sibling to cycle 197 panic's §default-erroneous-exit-no-further-loss-in-security argument (both designs §name-the-trade-off-explicitly); §forward-looking-statement-naming-future-flexibility ("In the future, this model can support multiple concurrent interaction paths, potentially including direct EVM user wallet access behind a timelock, without changing where assets are held"); §sibling to cycle 204 weblet-next's §Note-on-the-Next-Rendition and cycle 198 patterns-diagnostic-feedback's §future-helpers-named-not-shipped; §deposits-as-intents via Permit2 signed transfers with §factory-principal-check (only Ymax orchestration can spend); §router-acts-as-Permit2-spender; §the-factory's-deterministic-address-computation anchors deposit to portfolio account designated by authenticated Ymax instruction; §self-dispatch-pattern: instructions are encoded as calldata; target methods (while public on router) verify msg.sender == address(this); §two-named-benefits: (1) error-isolation-via-event-not-revert (router records failure in result event allowing messaging layer to mark delivery as consumed so it cannot be retried later); (2) block-explorer-readability (transaction easier to inspect; instruction decodable as function call instead of opaque bytes payload); §calldata-rewrite-replacing-txId-with-authenticated-source-address: "its encoding is defined to have the same length as the source address, and before dispatching, the router replaces it in the encoded calldata with the authenticated source address"; §borrowable-pattern: §converts-out-of-band-authentication-result-into-in-band-argument-that-self-dispatched-function-can-validate-naturally; §resolvers-as-bounded-fact-reporters: "resolvers are not trusted to report arbitrary EVM state. They are trusted only to submit one bounded fact back to the Agoric chain: whether a specific routed instruction settled as successful or failed"; §finality-checks-before-settlement defends against chain reorgs + relayer retries; §return-path-independent-of-outgoing-cross-chain-messaging-transport; §sibling to cycle 162 daemon-Ken-protocol's §atomic-checkpoint and cycle 197 panic's §prepare-commit-transactional-pattern (three designs at three layers using §all-or-none-discipline); §CREATE2-vs-CREATE3-distinction with named-rationale: permissionless-CREATE2 for Remote-Account-implementation (no per-chain init data); permissioned-CREATE3 for Axelar-router (per-chain init data like Axelar gateway address); permissioned-CREATE3 for Remote-Account-factory (currently uniform init data but future chains may differ for initial multisig vetting authority); §compromise-of-deployment-key-doesn't-alter-deployed-contract-behavior (only interferes with consistent-cross-chain-addresses goal); §OperationResult-event-common-format with txId + expected-target-account-address; §routed-instruction-lifecycle seven-named-steps: (1) authenticated-cross-chain-message arrives with sourceAddress + encoded-instruction; (2) validate-message (check instruction selector; decode common fields); (3) patch-calldata (insert authenticated sourceAddress replacing txId); (4) self-dispatch the patched calldata; (5) instruction-checks (msg.sender == address(this); source matches expected target); (6) perform-operation; (7) emit-OperationResult (txId + success/failure) OR revert-as-SubcallOutOfGas; §the-factory-IS-the-mediator (Remote Accounts statically defer to factory for router checks; factory maintains authorization state); §atomic-authorization-of-existing-accounts-to-new-router without per-account migration or asset movement; §Atredis-Partners-2026-audit with named-findings: zero critical/high/medium/low; three informational findings (two on consistent source-address formatting expectations across routers and factories; third on logic bug in router's out-of-gas heuristic handling — fixed in router implementation); §five-mermaid-diagrams: (1) orchestrated flow User → UI → Agoric → Base RA → Ethereum RA → Compound; (2) factory-portfolio mapping with CREATE2 clone salted by portfolio account; (3) authorized instruction flow Ymax → cross-chain messaging → EVM router → Remote Account; (4) router authorization state machine three-state with named transitions; (5) routed instruction lifecycle with sourceAddress + encoded-instruction → validate → patch → self-dispatch → checks → perform → emit-or-revert; §many-sibling-references to existing library cycles — §the-rhyme-is-strong across substrate technologies (EVM/Solidity vs Endo); §confirmation that §the-discipline-of-good-architecture-design transcends specific-substrate-technologies; §tier-1 borrowing: §assets-should-outlive-execution-paths + §three-named-properties-preserved + §EIP-1167-minimal-proxy for per-instance-addresses-sharing-implementation + §deterministic-address-derivation-from-multiple-identities (multi-tier identity anchoring) + §race-tolerant-idempotent-provisioning via the-deterministic-address-IS-the-coordination-primitive + §routers-as-authenticated-instruction-delivery-not-dumb-pipes with confused-deputy-defense + §three-state-router-authorization-state-machine with two-factor-mechanism + §honest-disclosure-of-residual-trust-boundaries + §forward-looking-statement-naming-future-flexibility + §permit-as-intent with factory-principal-check + §self-dispatch-pattern with msg.sender==address(this) for two-named-benefits + §calldata-rewrite-replacing-routing-field-with-authenticated-source + §resolvers-as-bounded-fact-reporters + §finality-checks-before-settlement + §decoupled-command-and-result-transports + §CREATE2-vs-CREATE3-distinction with named-per-chain-init-data-handling + §compromise-of-deployment-key-doesn't-alter-deployed-contract-behavior + §OperationResult-event-common-format + §routed-instruction-lifecycle with seven-named-steps + §the-factory-IS-the-mediator + §atomic-authorization-of-existing-accounts-to-new-router + §independent-audit-section-with-named-findings + §fix-status-per-finding + §five-mermaid-diagrams as visual-explanation-density; §synthesis-target: slot machine library §stable-game-state-containers-with-replaceable-control-paths — assets-should-outlive-execution-paths directly applicable (game-state-containers should be stable-objects with replaceable-engines); deterministic-address-derivation for game-state-anchored-to-player-identity + game-instance-namespace; three-state-router-authorization for game-rule-upgrade-paths; two-factor-mechanism for authority-staging-with-different-keys (dev-team-vets + game-operator-via-existing-rule-set-authorizes); self-dispatch-pattern for game-action-isolation (failed-action-as-event-not-game-state-revert); resolvers-as-bounded-fact-reporters for game-outcome-reporting that doesn't-trust-arbitrary-state; Permit2-as-intent for player-signed-bet-intents that game-engine-spends-as-permit-spender; Agoric Labs authored)

### From endojs/endo packages/hex/{src/encode.js, src/decode.js, index.js, README.md} (Cycle 215 chat-lane 2026-06-07; 172 source lines + ~80 README; @endo/hex is a §ponyfill for the TC39 `Uint8Array.prototype.toHex` / `Uint8Array.fromHex` intrinsics (proposal-arraybuffer-base64, Stage 4); on engines that ship the native intrinsics, the package dispatches to them at module load; on older engines and in SES-locked-down compartments that have removed the intrinsics, the package falls through to a portable pure-JS implementation; **single most structurally interesting move**: §native-error-rerun-polyfill-for-better-diagnostic — on native `Uint8Array.fromHex` throw, re-runs `jsDecodeHex(string, name)` to produce a precise-offset diagnostic with the caller's `name` parameter; falls back to native error if polyfill disagrees; the fast-path-success-path stays cheap (one native call); the error path pays the §quadratic-overhead-but-only-on-failure to get the precise offset and the user-provided `name` in the error message; §the-polyfill-is-the-error-formatter-not-just-the-fallback; §three-named-properties: (1) native-fast-path-stays-fast (no instrumentation overhead); (2) error-path-gets-precise-offset-diagnostic from the polyfill that knows ASCII offsets; (3) if-polyfill-disagrees-with-native (the polyfill would accept what native rejected), fall-back-to-the-native-error (`throw err`); §implementation-defined-native-error-messages-do-not-report-the-failing-offset, so this is not a redundant courtesy — it's the only way to give the user "invalid hex character at offset N of string `<name>`" diagnostics under a native-dispatched binding; §two-different-shapes-for-dispatching-to-native: unconditional for encode (cannot fail on valid input) + dispatch-with-on-failure-polyfill-rerun for decode (can fail on invalid input); §the-asymmetry-is-meaningful: §encode-cannot-fail-on-valid-input but §decode-can-fail-on-invalid-input; §the-polyfill-only-runs-on-failure-when-the-extra-information-is-actually-needed; §ponyfill-with-load-time-dispatch — the §dispatch-decision-is-made-once-at-module-load via IIFE-style pattern: both `encode.js` and `decode.js` read the native intrinsic at top level; §the-exported-binding-is-set-once at module evaluation; no per-call branching, no post-load reconfiguration; §the-public-API-is-just-encodeHex / `decodeHex`; the pure-JS fallbacks `jsEncodeHex` / `jsDecodeHex` are §also-exported-for-benchmarking-and-for-environments-without-the-native-intrinsic; §pre-lockdown-capture defense (SES interaction) — captures `Uint8Array.prototype.toHex` and `Uint8Array.fromHex` before SES lockdown freezes the prototype; post-lockdown mutation cannot redirect the dispatched binding; §capture-pre-lockdown-then-rely-on-immutability is the same shape `@endo/env-options` (cycle 207) uses for primordials capture, and the same shape the SES error-handling cluster uses; eleventh §SES-defense-family member in the library: §race-against-lockdown-to-snapshot-intrinsics + §post-lockdown-freezing-makes-the-snapshot-load-bearing; §capture-Reflect.apply-once-at-module-load — the fourth canonical uncurry shape; adds a fourth concrete instance to the §three-canonical-uncurry-shapes-in-endo lineage (cycle 199 trampoline-memoize-nat trio `bind.bind(bind.call)` + cycle 207 env-options `Reflect.apply` + cycle 211 @endo/common `Function.prototype.call.bind` + cycle 215 @endo/hex `Reflect.apply` revisit); §the-comment-is-explicit-about-why: *we prefer it to `Function.prototype.call` even where `.call` is assumed to be primordial, so a tampered `Function.prototype.call` cannot redirect the dispatched native intrinsic invocation*; §Reflect.apply-as-the-defensive-uncurry-against-Function.prototype.call-tampering; §pure-JS-fallback design choices: `jsEncodeHex` (60 lines) — §pre-allocate-the-output-array (`new Array(bytes.length * 2)`) §to-avoid-quadratic-time-string-concatenation; §index-into-a-16-character-alphabet-string for nibble→char mapping; final `chars.join('')` keeps a single string allocation; §lowercase-only with §caller-uppercases-if-needed as the explicit API contract; `jsDecodeHex` (112 lines) — §direct-nibble-computation-from-charcodes (no lookup table); §benchmark-result-noted-in-comment with §benchmark-file-named (`test/decode.bench.js` for the variants): *Computes nibble values directly from character codes rather than indexing a lookup table. On V8 (Node 22), this is roughly 2.5 to 3 times faster than the table-based decoder for ~1 MiB inputs and avoids any module-scope mutable data*; §XS-different-tradeoff-noted: *On XS the polyfill is unavoidably slow regardless of approach; see `test/decode.bench.js` for variants and the relative trade-offs. XS consumers should always reach the native `Uint8Array.fromHex` intrinsic dispatched below as soon as Moddable ships it*; §document-where-the-polyfill-is-known-to-be-slow + §point-at-the-native-intrinsic-as-the-eventual-answer; §`c | 0x20`-fold-uppercase-onto-lowercase trick with §range-check-still-rejects-bit-folded-non-letters — *For ASCII codes: '0' to '9' (48 to 57) -> c - 48; 'a' to 'f' / 'A' to 'F' (97/65 ..) -> (c | 0x20) - 87; `c | 0x20` folds upper- onto lowercase; non-letters with that bit set still fail the (97..102) range check below*; §the-bitwise-OR-doesn't-open-the-door-to-false-positives because §the-range-check-after-the-fold-is-restrictive; §name-for-error-diagnostics parameter — §optional-name-defaults-to-'<unknown>'; the name appears in error messages (`Hex string must have an even length, got ${string.length} in string ${name}` and `Invalid hex character at offset ${i * 2} of string ${name}`); §caller-supplied-context-string for §debugging-which-file-or-stream-the-bad-hex-came-from; §diagnostic-feedback-pattern that complements the cycle 198 §patterns-diagnostic-feedback work — both designs say §the-data-is-already-there-just-locked-include-it-in-the-error; §harden-every-export — `harden(jsEncodeHex);` `harden(jsDecodeHex);` `harden(encodeHex);` `harden(decodeHex);`; §every-exported-function-is-hardened (the polyfills, the dispatched defaults, all four); §belt-and-braces-against-tampering even for the polyfills (which §don't-need-to-be-hard-for-security but §are-hard-for-consistency-with-the-public-API); §compared-to-@endo/base64 (already ingested in cycle 7): @endo/base64 was pre-TC39-proposal native intrinsics + pure JS only (no native fallback) + throws on invalid chars (no offset) + single implementation; @endo/hex is §ponyfill-for-Stage-4-proposal + §load-time-dispatch-to-native-when-present + §precise-offset-diagnostic + §name-parameter + §double-decode-on-native-error; §two-different-binary-encoding-utility-shapes-in-the-same-library-family;
